title: "Attacks in Russia during the Russo-Ukrainian war (2022–present)"
---

# Attacks in Russia during the Russo-Ukrainian war (2022–present)

| Attacks in Russia during the Russo-Ukrainian war (2022–present) |  |
| --- | --- |
| Part of the [Russo-Ukrainian war](70149799.md) |  |
| The May 2023 [Kremlin drone attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kremlin_drone_attack) |  |
| Location | [Russia](25391.md) |
| Date | 25 February 2022 (2022-02-25) – present (4 years, 7 months and 8 days) |
| Executed by | [Ministry of Defence](14284651.md)  [Armed Forces of Ukraine](42799055.md)  [Unmanned Systems Forces](76045069.md)[^cite_note-1]  [Ukrainian Navy](1590554.md)[^cite_note-2]  [Main Directorate of Intelligence](1406269.md) (HUR)  [International Legion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Legion_of_the_Defence_Intelligence_of_Ukraine)  [Freedom of Russia Legion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_Russia_Legion)  [Russian Volunteer Corps](71652852.md)  [Security Service of Ukraine](709222.md) (SBU)  [Combat Organization of Anarcho-Communists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combat_Organization_of_Anarcho-Communists) |
| Casualties | 621+ civilians killed[^cite_note-3] unknown military deaths |

There have been attacks in mainland [Russia](25391.md) as a result of the [Russo-Ukrainian war since its invasion of Ukraine on 24 February 2022](70149799.md). The main targets have been the [military](25709.md), the [arms industry](12513462.md) and the [oil industry](10450931.md). Many of the attacks have been [drone](53703349.md) strikes, firebombing, and [rail sabotage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_war_in_Russia_\(2022%E2%80%93present\)). The Ukrainian intelligence services have acknowledged carrying out some of these attacks. Others have been carried out by [anti-war activists in Russia](70159281.md). There have also been cross-border shelling, missile strikes, and covert raids from Ukraine, mainly in [Belgorod](478857.md), [Kursk](480916.md), and [Bryansk](476473.md) [oblasts](646268.md). Several times, Ukrainian-based paramilitaries launched incursions into Russia, captured border villages and battled the Russian military. These were carried out by units made up mainly of [Russian emigrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russians_in_Ukraine). While Ukraine supported these ground incursions, it denied direct involvement.&#x20;

In August 2024, the Ukrainian military launched an [offensive into Kursk Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kursk_campaign) and captured numerous settlements. It was the biggest attack into Russia since the war began and the first carried out primarily by Ukrainian regular forces.&#x20;

In response to the war, there have also been [attacks on Russian forces in Crimea](71954377.md), which Russia annexed in 2014.&#x20;

## Shelling, drone strikes and sabotage

### February–March 2022

Further information: [Millerovo air base attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millerovo_air_base_attack)

On 25 February 2022, the [Millerovo air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millerovo_\(air_base\)) was [attacked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millerovo_air_base_attack), allegedly with [Tochka-U](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OTR-21_Tochka) missiles.[^cite_note-4] One [Su-30](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Su-30)SM fighter was destroyed on the ground.[^cite_note-5]&#x20;

On 1 March, there was an explosion on a military air base in [Taganrog](1288065.md) in [Rostov Oblast](478744.md). There were claims that it was due to Ukrainian action.[^cite_note-6]&#x20;

On 23 and 24 March, [Governor of Belgorod Oblast](68851709.md) [Vyacheslav Gladkov](67064358.md) reported that [Zhuravlyovka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhuravlyovka,_Belgorod_Oblast) and [Nekhoteyevka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nekhoteyevka) came under shelling from the Ukrainian side. The next day, the [Moscow Patriarchate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Church) claimed that [chaplain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaplain) Oleg Artyomov died in Zhuravlyovka as a result of a [BM-30 Smerch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BM-30_Smerch) strike by Ukraine.[^cite_note-7]&#x20;

On 29 March, local officials reported a series of explosions outside the city of [Belgorod](478861.md), close to the border with [Ukraine](31750.md). It was later reported that those explosions may have been caused by a fire.[^cite_note-8]&#x20;

### April 2022

On 1 April 2022, according to regional governor [Vyacheslav Gladkov](67064358.md) and an unnamed US official, two Ukrainian [Mi-24](384591.md) helicopters attacked and set fire to a fuel depot in Belgorod in a low-altitude airstrike with no reported casualties.[^cite_note-9][^cite_note-10][^cite_note-11] Ukraine denied and dismissed the event as Russian propaganda.[^cite_note-12][^cite_note-timesofisrael.com-13] Ukrainian security official [Oleksiy Danilov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleksiy_Danilov) denied Ukraine was behind the helicopter attack,[^cite_note-14][^cite_note-timesofisrael.com-13] with a joke in which he blamed the "People's Republic of Belgorod" instead.[^cite_note-15][^cite_note-16][^cite_note-17] On the same day, a rocket exploded in a different part of the oblast, but its apparent trajectory and model led open-source researchers to suspect it was a failed Russian missile.[^cite_note-18]&#x20;

On 11 April, Belgorod, Bryansk, [Kursk](480916.md), and [Voronezh](473401.md) Oblasts all raised their [terror alert system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terror_alert_system#Russia) to "[yellow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellow)", the second tier in a three-class system.[^cite_note-Russian_threat-19][^cite_note-20] The [Republic of Crimea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Crimea_\(Russia\)) and [Krasnodar Krai](474125.md) raised their alert levels in certain districts. Authorities in Belgorod ordered a two-week ban on [fireworks](59493.md) and [firecrackers](418188.md).[^cite_note-Russian_threat-19]&#x20;

On 14 April 2022, the [FSB border service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FSB_Border_Service_of_Russia) reported that on 13 April, a border checkpoint near [Novye Yurkovichi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novye_Yurkovichi), [Bryansk Oblast](476473.md) came under mortar fire from Ukraine while a group of around 30 Ukrainian refugees headed for Russia was present there. According to the official claims, two automobiles were damaged but no injuries were documented.[^cite_note-21][^cite_note-AFP-22][^cite_note-bryansk_shelling-23][^cite_note-euronews.com-24]&#x20;

On the same day, regional and municipal authorities stated that Ukraine had shelled the village of [Spodaryushino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spodaryushino) (near [Mokraya Orlovka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mokraya_Orlovka)) in [Belgorod Oblast](478857.md), causing several explosions. While no injuries occurred, the village's population was temporarily evacuated, out of concerns about a possible escalation. A neighboring settlement also had its population relocated.[^cite_note-25] [Governor of Belgorod Oblast](68851709.md) [Vyacheslav Gladkov](67064358.md) said that the attack "had come from the Ukrainian side."[^cite_note-AFP-22] In a separate alleged attack a resident of Zhuravlyovka was injured, according to Gladkov.[^cite_note-26][^cite_note-27]&#x20;

Also on 14 April, the [Investigative Committee of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Investigative_Committee_of_Russia) said Ukrainian attack helicopters had launched six missile strikes on residential areas in the town of [Klimovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klimovo,_Bryansk_Oblast), [Bryansk Oblast](476473.md), damaging six buildings.[^cite_note-28][^cite_note-helicopter_strike-29] Officials at the [Russian Health Ministry](29896200.md) said that seven people had been injured, two of which had been hurt seriously.[^cite_note-helicopter_strike-29] According to personnel at the city's hospital, among those injured were a pregnant woman and a two-year-old child.[^cite_note-bryansk_shelling-23][^cite_note-AFP-22] According to [Radio Free Europe/Radio Liberty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Free_Europe/Radio_Liberty), an unverified video of a house in Bryansk burning [went viral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viral_phenomenon) on the internet.[^cite_note-bryansk_shelling-23] The next day, Russian security services claimed they had shot down a [Mil Mi-8](957233.md) helicopter during the incident.[^cite_note-Kyiv_strikes-30]&#x20;

On 15 April, Russia launched major missile strikes on the Ukrainian capital [Kyiv](585629.md) in retaliation for alleged cross-border attacks.[^cite_note-Kyiv_strikes-30] The Russian strikes were the largest of their kind to take place since Russia aborted its 2022 [Kyiv offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_front_of_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine).[^cite_note-31]&#x20;

Ukraine rejected claims that it was responsible for the 14 April 2022 attacks, instead asserting that Russian intelligence services were trying to "carry out terrorist acts to whip up anti-Ukrainian hysteria" in the country.[^cite_note-32][^cite_note-helicopter_strike-29] After the attack, the [Security Service of Ukraine](709222.md) released what it claimed were intercepted conversations between Russian soldiers showing that Russia deliberately fired on the villages in order to blame Ukraine. One of the alleged soldiers refers to the [1999 Russian apartment bombings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_Russian_apartment_bombings) where he says that the "same shit happened in the Chechen war. Apartments were blown up in Moscow, like they were terrorists. In fact, they are FSB officers".[^cite_note-33] Ukrainian interior ministry advisor [Anton Gerashchenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anton_Gerashchenko) said that something "fell and caught fire" at a Russian military facility, without explicitly confirming or denying Ukrainian responsibility.[^cite_note-euronews.com-24]&#x20;

On 19 April, Belgorod's governor accused Ukrainian forces of striking the village of [Golovchino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golovchino), damaging more than 30 houses and lightly wounding 3 residents.[^cite_note-34] On 25 April, according to the Belgorod governor, at least two residents, a man and a woman, were injured in [Zhuravlyovka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhuravlyovka,_Belgorod_Oblast) as a result of shelling.[^cite_note-35]&#x20;

On the same day, another attack happened in [Bryansk](476476.md): in the morning, two large explosions and fires occurred at two oil facilities, a civilian one and a military one. Videos and images posted on social media showed large columns of black smoke several hours after the initial explosions. An analyst told *The Guardian* that the fires were likely an act of [sabotage](29462.md) by Ukraine, although responsibility remained uncertain.[^cite_note-36][^cite_note-37] Unconfirmed reports in the Russian media suggested the fires could have been caused by a [drone](58900.md) attack; on the same day, two [Bayraktar TB2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayraktar_TB2) drones were reportedly shot down in [Bryansk Oblast](476473.md).[^cite_note-38]&#x20;

On 29–30 April, a border checkpoint near Krupets in [Rylsky District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rylsky_District) of Kursk Oblast was repeatedly shelled, according to the governor. The Bryansk governor said that his region was shelled as well.[^cite_note-39][^cite_note-40]&#x20;

### May 2022

On 11 May, the Belgorod governor claimed that [Solokhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solokhi) was shelled from Ukraine. According to him, one person was killed and seven were injured as a result of the incident.[^cite_note-41] Videos online also showed damage to stores and private houses, local officials reportedly began evacuating the village after the attack.[^cite_note-42] The deceased was identified as Ruslan Nefyodov, aged 18.[^cite_note-43]&#x20;

On 15 May, Gladkov said that one person was injured with a shrapnel wound in [Sereda](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Sereda,_Belgorod_Oblast\&action=edit\&redlink=1) after a Ukrainian strike,[^cite_note-44] as 10 shells were reportedly shot down by Russian air defense systems. Another 10 shells fell close to [Novostroyevka-Vtoraya](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Novostroyevka-Vtoraya\&action=edit\&redlink=1), while another round of eight artillery shells reportedly damaged a power line and a number of farming structures in Zhuravlyovka.[^cite_note-45]&#x20;

On 17 May, Gladkov claimed that one more person was slightly injured in [Bezymeno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bezymeno) as it was shelled from the territory of Ukraine.[^cite_note-Novaya-46] Kursk governor said that the border checkpoint at [Tyotkino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyotkino) had been shelled once again on the same day, no victims were reported.[^cite_note-Novaya-46] On 18 May, Gladkov declared that Solokhi was shelled again, and one person was reportedly injured.[^cite_note-47] Kursk governor Starovoyt reported that [Alekseevka, Glushkovsky District](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Alekseevka,_Glushkovsky_District\&action=edit\&redlink=1), was shelled.[^cite_note-48] On 19 May, Starovoyt claimed that a distillery in Tyotkino was shelled, a truck driver died, and at least one civilian was wounded.[^cite_note-49][^cite_note-50][^cite_note-51] On the same day, Alekseevka and [Dronovka](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Dronovka,_Glushkovsky_District\&action=edit\&redlink=1) were reportedly shelled.[^cite_note-52]&#x20;

On 25 May, Gladkov claimed that one person was wounded in [Zhuravlyovka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhuravlyovka,_Belgorod_Oblast) as it was shelled "from the territory of Ukraine".[^cite_note-53] Zhuravlyovka and [Nekhoteyevka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nekhoteyevka) were subject to continuous shelling on 26 May. A woman died from her wounds in the hospital the next day.[^cite_note-54][^cite_note-55][^cite_note-56] On 26 May, Starovoyt said that [Vorozhba, Sudzhansky District](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Vorozhba,_Sudzhansky_District\&action=edit\&redlink=1) came under shelling "from the Ukrainian side", and a local school teacher was wounded by broken glass.[^cite_note-57]&#x20;

### June–July 2022

On 6 June, a bridge at Tyotkino was shelled, damaging it. Near the bridge, a block of apartments was badly damaged, a car was burnt out and the local [sugar refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugar_refinery) suffered some damage.[^cite_note-58]&#x20;

[Klintsy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klintsy) and its surrounding regions in Bryansk Oblast were shelled for three days straight beginning on 12 June. The first two days of attacks were more minor incidents targeting military bases, with little to no casualties. On 14 June a helicopter fired missiles at residential areas in the city, damaging dozens of houses and ripping a woman's leg off.[^cite_note-59] Authorities recorded six injuries on 14 June.[^cite_note-60] As a result of the attacks, Klintsy and the surrounding region was left without water and electricity.[^cite_note-61]&#x20;

On 22 June, the [Novoshakhtinsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novoshakhtinsk) oil refinery in [Rostov Oblast](478744.md) was reportedly hit by a [suicide drone](53703349.md), no casualties were reported.[^cite_note-62]&#x20;

The [Combat Organization of Anarcho-Communists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combat_Organization_of_Anarcho-Communists) sabotaged a railway line near Moscow on 25 June, to hinder the supply of equipment to a military base. They issued a statement online saying: "Every stopped train means less shells and rockets to fly into peaceful Ukrainian cities ... We call on everyone to join the [rail war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_war_in_Russia_\(2022%E2%80%93present\))!".[^cite_note-63]&#x20;

On 3 July, officials said that a group of explosions targeting civilian areas in Belgorod had killed at least five people and injured four, several buildings and private houses were damaged or destroyed, anti-aircraft defense systems reportedly activated, but one projectile hit an apartment building.[^cite_note-64][^cite_note-65] Russia blamed Ukraine for the attack and Defence Ministry spokesman [Igor Konashenkov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Igor_Konashenkov) said that Russia had shot down three Ukrainian [Tochka U](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tochka_U) [ballistic missiles](38025.md) armed with [cluster warheads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cluster_warhead). Ukraine denied it was responsible.[^cite_note-66][^cite_note-67][^cite_note-68]&#x20;

### September–October 2022

A civilian was killed and two hospitalized after shelling hit the town of [Valuyki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valuyki,_Belgorod_Oblast) in [Belgorod Oblast](478857.md) on 15 September, according to regional authorities.[^cite_note-69][^cite_note-70] Earlier, Russian authorities had reported that a border checkpoint on Nekhoteevka was attacked and that the customs terminal had caught on fire.[^cite_note-71]&#x20;

On 11 October, an explosion was reported at an electric substation in Shebekino; some residents were left without energy.[^cite_note-72] On 15 October, new explosions were reported in Belgorod; an oil depot reportedly caught on fire.[^cite_note-73] On 16 October, explosions were reported at an airport in Belgorod; videos online show anti-aircraft systems firing and smoke rising on the airport after an apparent missile strike, with two people reportedly being wounded.[^cite_note-74]&#x20;

### November 2022

On 1 November, the settlement of Krasnooktyabrsky in [Glushkovsky District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glushkovsky_District) was reportedly shelled by Ukrainian forces; no injuries were reported, a five-story building was damaged and several windows were blown. Another attack was reported in Kozynka; one person was reportedly wounded in the shelling.[^cite_note-75] On 2 November 5 civilians, including 3 children, were reportedly injured after shells hit the village of [Guyevo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guyevo), [Sudzhansky District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sudzhansky_District). Several private houses and stores were damaged in the attack; the village was left without electricity as power lines were either destroyed or damaged following the explosions.[^cite_note-76]&#x20;

On 14 November, Russian media reported several blasts in the Belgorod region.[^cite_note-77] On 15 November 2 civilians were killed and 3 injured by shelling in Shebekino, Belgorod Oblast; the shells reportedly hit apartment buildings and a nearby pharmacy.[^cite_note-78] The governor of Belgorod said that an 80-year-old woman died of her wounds in the hospital and that a man was killed and another was injured by parts of a missile that was shot down by air defense.[^cite_note-79] On 16 November, in the village of Stalnoy Kon, [Oryol Oblast](480945.md), an [unmanned aerial vehicle](58900.md) reportedly attacked an oil depot; no casualties were reported.&#x20;

### December 2022

Further information: [Dyagilevo and Engels air bases attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dyagilevo_and_Engels_air_bases_attacks)

On 5 December, two air bases reported explosions, allegedly caused by Ukrainian drone attacks. At [Engels-2 air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Engels-2_\(air_base\)) in [Saratov Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saratov_Oblast), 2 [Tu-95s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tu-95) were reportedly damaged. At [Dyagilevo air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dyagilevo_\(air_base\)) in [Ryazan Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryazan_Oblast), an oil truck exploded, killing three soldiers and injuring four.[^cite_note-80] On 6 December, [Governor of Kursk Oblast](70945112.md) [Roman Starovoyt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Starovoyt) announced that an oil reservoir near [Kursk-Khalino air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kursk_Vostochny_Airport) caught on fire as a result of a drone attack; no casualties were reported and the fire was quickly put out.[^cite_note-81] Following the alleged Ukrainian drone strikes, Russian forces launched a new wave of [attacks against Ukrainian cities](71963495.md).[^cite_note-82] A missile reportedly fell on Moldova as a result of the Russian attack.[^cite_note-83]&#x20;

On 17 December, explosions were reported in Belgorod, a local resident's car was reportedly damaged by a fragment of ammunition debris.[^cite_note-84] On 18 December, explosions were reported again in Belgorod, officials claimed that they were the result of anti-aircraft fire; several cars and houses were damaged by debris, 4 people were wounded in the city itself, and 1 person was killed in the suburbs, local Telegram channels also posted images showing thick black smoke rising over the city.[^cite_note-18December-85]&#x20;

More explosions were reported elsewhere in Belgorod Oblast, a poultry farm was reportedly hit. Two people, including an employee of the farm, died as a result of the explosions, three other employees were also wounded during the alleged attack; local Telegram channels showed videos allegedly showing the aftermath of the attack.[^cite_note-18December-85] On 25 December, three soldiers were killed in another attack on Engels air base.[^cite_note-86]&#x20;

### January–March 2023

On 4 January 2023, the [Combat Organization of Anarcho-Communists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combat_Organization_of_Anarcho-Communists) claimed responsibility for an explosion that damaged the [Trans-Siberian Railway](56912.md) in [Krasnoyarsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krasnoyarsk_Krai). The rail line was used by the Russian Army to transport supplies to occupied Ukraine.[^cite_note-87]&#x20;

On 11 February, the Governor of the Belgorod Oblast reported that Ukrainian [Grad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BM-21_Grad) missiles had hit the city of [Shebekino](4570394.md), and that as a result of the attack, three men had been wounded, one building of an industrial enterprise had been destroyed and a bus stop, as well as three private cars, had been damaged. Another attack with Grad rockets was reported later, with local authorities claiming that the missiles had hit two private houses, one of which caught on fire as result of the strike.[^cite_note-88]&#x20;

On 22 February, regional authorities reported that two people had been injured by shelling in Shebekino, and that three houses were damaged from shelling in [Pervoye Tseplyayevo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pervoye_Tseplyayevo).[^cite_note-89] On 28 February, a [Rosneft](1313566.md) oil depot in [Tuapse](1623160.md), [Krasnodar Krai](474125.md), caught on fire after an alleged drone attack. Locals reported several explosions in the depot, which local officials blamed on two drones that allegedly crashed into the terminal and exploded. The boiler room was reportedly damaged, although the local government denied any damage to the facility's oil tanks; there were no victims, and the fire was quickly extinguished.[^cite_note-90] On the same day, an FSB border service observation tower was damaged by an UAV, allegedly from Ukraine.[^cite_note-91]&#x20;

On 4 March, a drone carrying an improvised explosive device attacked a substation of the Transneft-Druzhba oil pipeline in Belgorod Oblast. Four people were evacuated, but no injuries were reported.[^cite_note-92] On 6 March, more attacks were reported in Kursk and Belgorod Oblasts. Starovoyt claimed the village of Iskra suffered power outages after energy facilities reported damages during the attack. According to Belgorod Oblast Governor Vyacheslav Gladkov, in the town of [Novy Oskol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novy_Oskol), although three missiles were shot down, one civilian was injured and several buildings in the town were damaged, in addition to power outages.[^cite_note-93]&#x20;

On 15 March, two missiles were downed by air defense systems over the city of Belgorod, according to Belgorod Oblast Governor Gladkov, with no injuries or damage reported.[^cite_note-94] On 27 March, a drone attacked a gas station in Belgorod late in the evening; no casualties were reported.[^cite_note-95]&#x20;

### April 2023

On 6 April, a drone with several cameras crashed in the [Fakel Machine-Building Design Bureau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MKB_Fakel) in [Khimki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khimki), [Moscow Oblast](372315.md); the drone was allegedly downed by communication jammers, and was found by security guards patrolling the region.[^cite_note-96] On 9 April, Belgorod Governor Gladkov claimed that Ukrainian forces had shelled the border town of [Voznesenovka](61807074.md), Shebekinsky District; two houses and one farm were damaged by shells, but no casualties were reported.[^cite_note-97]&#x20;

On 10 April, a drone carrying a bomb crashed into a fence at Belgorod airport, no casualties were reported, but the explosion damaged the airport's fence and alarm system.[^cite_note-98] On 16 April, a drone attacked an [electrical substation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electrical_substation) in Belgorod, no casualties were reported.[^cite_note-99]&#x20;

### May 2023

Further information: [Kremlin drone attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kremlin_drone_attack) and [30 May 2023 Moscow drone strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/30_May_2023_Moscow_drone_strikes)

On 3 May, Russia said it had shot down two drones over the [Moscow Kremlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kremlin) and accused Ukraine of sending them to assassinate President Vladimir Putin.[^cite_note-100] The Ukrainian government denied the accusations, calling them fabricated.[^cite_note-101] Also on 3 May, several explosions and a fire were reported early on the morning at the [Port of Taman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Taman), near the village of Volna, Krasnodar Krai, images show flames and smoke over what is believed to be large fuel tanks, no casualties were reported, Russian media outlets blamed the explosions and subsequent fire on an alleged Ukrainian drone attack; the port is about 15 km from the [Crimean Bridge](22563317.md) and is used as a logistic hub for operations in Southern Ukraine.[^cite_note-102]&#x20;

Another attack was reported on the same day in Bryansk Oblast, where five Ukrainian drones allegedly attacked a military airfield. Two drones reportedly hit their targets on the airfield, whereas two were destroyed and one was not found. No casualties were reported, and one unoperated An-124 military transport aircraft was slightly damaged during the attack, according to local media outlets.[^cite_note-103]&#x20;

On 4 May, early in the morning, an unidentified drone struck the territory of the military unit No. 45117 in the town of Buturlinovka, Voronezh Oblast; this was followed by another alleged Ukrainian drone attack at the Novoshakhtinsk oil refinery in the village of Kiselyovka, Rostov Oblast; the drone crashed and exploded at the Refinery's inter-shop flyover, which was under construction. The fire was quickly put out and no casualties were reported.[^cite_note-104]&#x20;

On 10 May, an unidentified drone struck an administrative building in [Starodub](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starodub), Bryansk Oblast; another drone also dropped an explosive device on the [Klintsy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klintsy) oil depot (owned by Rosneft) in Bryansk proper on 11 May. The concrete base and oil products storage tank were partially damaged, while no casualties were reported; regional governor Alexander Bogomaz blamed Ukraine for the attacks.[^cite_note-105] On 14 May, an unidentified drone dropped an explosive device on the roof of a military warehouse in [Suzemka District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suzemsky_District) of Bryansk Oblast, near the border with Ukraine; there were no casualties, although some of the equipment there was damaged.[^cite_note-106]&#x20;

On 15 May, a drone allegedly crashed into a border post in Bryansk Oblast in the evening, injuring five border guards. Another attack was also reported in Sudzhansky District of Kursk Oblast; according to Governor Starovoyt, a drone dropped a bomb on a working excavator, inflicting minor injuries on the operator.[^cite_note-107] On 30 May, Moscow [was attacked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/30_May_2023_Moscow_drone_strikes) by drones.[^cite_note-108]&#x20;

### June–July 2023



Damage on the [IQ-quarter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IQ-quarter) housing government offices caused by a Ukrainian drone strike in Moscow, 30 July 2023

- 9 June: three people were reportedly injured when a drone struck a building in the city of [Voronezh](467218.md).

  [^cite_note-109]

- 19 June: the governor of Belgorod Oblast claimed that seven people were injured by shelling in [Valuyki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valuyki,_Belgorod_Oblast).

  [^cite_note-110]

- 2 July: a missile was shot down near [Primorsko-Akhtarsk air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primorsko-Akhtarsk_\(air_base\)) in [Krasnodar Krai](474125.md), leaving a large crater.

  [^cite_note-111]

- 4 July: Russia claimed to have shot down four drones outside Moscow while jamming a fifth with [electronic warfare](82272.md), forcing it to crash in the [Odintsovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odintsovo) district of [Moscow Oblast](372315.md). No casualties were reported. Flights from [Vnukovo International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vnukovo_International_Airport) were suspended.

  [^cite_note-112]

- 8–9 July: Russian forces claimed to have shot down four Ukrainian missiles over Belgorod,

  [^cite_note-113]

  &#x20;Bryansk and Rostov Oblasts, destroying a sawmill.

  [^cite_note-114]

- 16 July: a woman was reportedly killed by Ukrainian shelling in [Shebekino](4570394.md), Belgorod Oblast.

  [^cite_note-115]

- 28 July: A missile was shot down over the city of [Taganrog](1288065.md), and another near [Azov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azov), Rostov Oblast. Fourteen people were injured. A bomb was detonated at the [Kuibyshev oil refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuibyshev_refinery) in [Samara Oblast](481962.md).

  [^cite_note-116]

- 30 July: A Ukrainian drone strike on Moscow damaged the [IQ-quarter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IQ-quarter) and [OKO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OKO) skyscrapers of the [MIBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moscow_International_Business_Center) and led to an hour-long suspension of operations at [Vnukovo International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vnukovo_International_Airport). The Russian military claimed to have intercepted three drones, two of which hit the buildings.

  [^cite_note-117]

- 31 July: a police station in Bryansk Oblast was reportedly hit by a drone.

  [^cite_note-118]

### August 2023

- 1 August:&#x20;

  - Several drones were shot down over Moscow, but one struck the IQ-quarter, which housed federal government offices. Vnukovo Airport was briefly closed again.

    [^cite_note-119]

    [^cite_note-120]

  - A military recruitment office in St Petersburg was set on fire by a man who claimed to have been contacted by the FSB to "gain access to documents sent to Ukraine". Russian media said it was the ninth such attack on recruitment centers across the country in recent days, which authorities attributed to "phone scammers".

    [^cite_note-121]

- 4 August:&#x20;

  - Ukrainian sources reported that Ukrainian drone boats severely damaged the *[Olenegorsky Gornyak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olenegorsky_Gornyak)*, a [Russian landing ship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ropucha-class_landing_ship), in a naval attack on [Novorossiysk](1190336.md), Krasnodar Krai.

    [^cite_note-122]

    [^cite_note-123]

  - two government buildings were reportedly damaged in a drone attack in [Kursk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kursk).

    [^cite_note-124]

- 16 August: Russia claimed to have "eliminated" a group of four Ukrainian saboteurs who entered Bryansk Oblast.

  [^cite_note-125]

- 18 August: a drone was shot down over Moscow's business district, crashing into and damaging the [Expocentre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expocentre).

  [^cite_note-126]

  [^cite_note-127]

- 19 August: a Russian [Tupolev Tu-22M](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tupolev_Tu-22M) bomber was destroyed by a drone strike at [Soltsy air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soltsy-2_\(air_base\)), [Novgorod Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novgorod_Oblast) in northwestern Russia.

  [^cite_note-128]

  &#x20;It was believed to be the first attack of its kind in the region.

  [^cite_note-129]

- 20 August: A drone reportedly set fire to the roof of [Kursk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kursk) railway station, injuring five people.

  [^cite_note-130]

  &#x20;More drones were downed in Rostov Oblast, Belgorod and Moscow, forcing the closure of [Vnukovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vnukovo_airport) and [Domodedovo airports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domodedovo_airport).

  [^cite_note-131]

  [^cite_note-132]

- 21 August: Two people were reportedly injured by wreckage from a drone shot down over Moscow. Some fifty flights were disrupted at [Vnukovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vnukovo_airport), [Domodedovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domodedovo_airport), [Sheremetyevo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheremetyevo_airport) and [Zhunovsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhukovsky_International_Airport) airports. Ukraine's [Main Intelligence Directorate](1406269.md) (the HUR) said it oversaw a drone attack on the [Shaykovka military air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaykovka_air_base) in [Kaluga Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaluga_Oblast).

  [^cite_note-133]

- 22 August: Two drones were shot down near Moscow, with one hitting a 25-story building in [Krasnogorsk](2399024.md), and forcing the closure of the city's major airports. Two other drones were shot down over Bryansk Oblast.

  [^cite_note-134]

- 23 August: The Governor of Belgorod Oblast said that a drone struck a sanatorium in the village of [Lavy](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lavy,_Belgorod_Oblast\&action=edit\&redlink=1), killing three. Another drone was downed over central Moscow, crashing into a high-rise building under construction, and forcing the closure of the city's airports.

  [^cite_note-135]

  [^cite_note-136]

- 25 August: The Russian Defense Ministry said it shot down an [S-200](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S-200_missile_system) missile near Shaykovka military air base in Kaluga Oblast. Explosions were also reported in [Tula Oblast](480930.md).

  [^cite_note-137]

- 26 August: Another drone was shot down near Moscow,

  [^cite_note-138]

  &#x20;prompting the closure of the city's airports.

  [^cite_note-139]

  &#x20;The governor of Belgorod Oblast claimed that a drone killed one person in the village of [Shchetinovka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shchetinovka),

  [^cite_note-140]

  &#x20;and that Ukrainian shelling damaged buildings and injured four in the village of [Urazovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urazovo),

  [^cite_note-141]

- 27 August: the [Security Service of Ukraine](709222.md) (SBU) said it launched sixteen drones in an overnight attack on a military air base in [Kursk Oblast](480916.md), which destroyed four Russian [Su-30](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Su-30) fighter jets and one [MIG-29](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MIG-29), and damaged anti-aircraft systems.

  [^cite_note-142]

- 30 August: Ukraine reportedly launched a wave of drone strikes overnight on six regions in western Russia. One struck [Pskov Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pskov_Airport), near the Estonian border, destroying two [Ilyushin Il-76](229943.md) transport aircraft and damaging two others. The raid was launched from within Russia, according to [Kyrylo Budanov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyrylo_Budanov), head of the Ukrainian [HUR](1406269.md).

  [^cite_note-143]

  [^cite_note-144]

  &#x20;Ukrainian officials, who did not claim responsibility for the attacks, claimed that a fuel depot in [Kaluga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaluga) and a microelectronics factory in [Bryansk](476476.md) were also struck.

  [^cite_note-145]

### September 2023

- 1 September: A drone reportedly damaged a factory that made components for [rockets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocket_artillery) in [Lyubertsy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyubertsy), [Moscow Oblast](372315.md),

  [^cite_note-146]

  &#x20;forcing the closure of airports in the area. At least two buildings were reportedly damaged in another drone strike in [Kurchatov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurchatov,_Russia), [Kursk Oblast](480916.md).

  [^cite_note-147]

- 2 September: the governor of Belgorod Oblast claimed that Ukrainian shelling killed one person in [Urazovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urazovo).

  [^cite_note-148]

- 7 September: Another wave of drone attacks was reported across Russia. In [Rostov-on-Don](317562.md), three buildings were damaged in a reported strike by two drones at the headquarters of the [Southern Military District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Military_District). Another drone fell on a military base in [Volgograd Oblast](478748.md),

  [^cite_note-149]

  &#x20;the first such incident in the region, while another drone started a fire at an industrial facility in [Bryansk](476476.md).

  [^cite_note-150]

- 17 September: a drone damaged an oil depot in [Oryol](488242.md).

  [^cite_note-151]

- 18 September: [Ukrainian military intelligence](1406269.md) claimed that a Russian [An-148](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/An-148), an [Ilyushin Il-20](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilyushin_Il-20) and a [Mil Mi-28](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mil_Mi-28) helicopter were damaged or destroyed in a sabotage attack at [Chkalovsky Air Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chkalovsky_Air_Base) near Moscow.

  [^cite_note-152]

- 20 September: a fuel storage tank caught fire near [Sochi airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sochi_International_Airport) following a suspected drone strike.

  [^cite_note-153]

- 24 September: The governor of Kursk Oblast claimed that a government building was damaged by a Ukrainian drone in [Kursk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kursk) city.

  [^cite_note-154]

  &#x20;Ukrainian military intelligence claimed that a drone strike on the [Khalino air field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kursk_Vostochny_Airport) in the same region killed or wounded the commander and other officers of the Russian 14th Guards Fighter Aviation Regiment.

  [^cite_note-155]

- 29 September: Russian officials said Ukrainian drones dropped explosives on an electrical substation in the village of [Belaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belaya,_Kursk_Oblast) in [Kursk Oblast](480916.md), cutting power to five settlements. A dozen other drones were reportedly shot down over Belgorod, Kursk and Kaluga Oblasts.

  [^cite_note-156]

  &#x20;A radar station near [Giryi](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Giryi\&action=edit\&redlink=1), Kursk Oblast was reportedly destroyed by a drone.

  [^cite_note-157]

### October 2023

- 1 October: Ukrainian exploding drones reportedly struck [Adler air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sochi_International_Airport) in [Sochi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sochi) and an aircraft factory operated by the state-run Tactical Missile Armament corporation in [Smolensk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smolensk).

  [^cite_note-158]

- 4 October: Russia claimed to have shot down 31 Ukrainian drones during an overnight raid on its western border regions.

  [^cite_note-159]

  &#x20;A Russian [S-400 missile system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S-400_missile_system) was reportedly struck by drones near Belgorod.

  [^cite_note-160]

- 7 October: One person was reportedly killed by Ukrainian shelling in [Urazovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urazovo), Belgorod Oblast. The Russian Defense Ministry claimed to have destroyed three [Tochka-U](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tochka-U) missiles over the region and thwarted a drone attack on Moscow.

  [^cite_note-161]

- 10 October: A couple was reportedly killed by Ukrainian shelling in [Popovka, Belgorod Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popovka,_Belgorod_Oblast).

  [^cite_note-162]

  &#x20;A Russian conscript was killed while five other soldiers were wounded in an attack in [Gudovka](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Gudovka\&action=edit\&redlink=1), Bryansk Oblast.

  [^cite_note-163]

- 12 October: two people were reportedly killed in a house fire caused by falling drone wreckage in Belgorod Oblast.

  [^cite_note-164]

- 15 October: Russia claimed to have shot down 27 drones over Kursk and Belgorod Oblasts.

  [^cite_note-165]

  &#x20;An attack on an energy facility in [Krasnaya Yaruga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krasnaya_Yaruga), Belgorod Oblast, caused blackouts in the area.

  [^cite_note-166]

- 18 October: Russia said it had shot down 28 drones over Kursk and Belgorod Oblasts as well as over the [Black Sea](3386.md).

  [^cite_note-167]

  &#x20;A military camp near [Khalino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khalino) air base in [Kursk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kursk) was reportedly struck by drones launched by Ukraine's SBU.

  [^cite_note-168]

- 26 October: Russia claimed that three drones tried to attack the Kursk Nuclear Power Plant.

  [^cite_note-169]

  &#x20;The FSB claimed to have killed a man in a shootout in [Tver](372316.md), who was working for Ukrainian intelligence.

  [^cite_note-170]

- 29 October: A drone caused a fire at the Afipsky oil refinery near [Novorossiysk](1190336.md).

  [^cite_note-171]

  &#x20;Ukrainian media reported that the attack was organized by the SBU.

  [^cite_note-172]

### November 2023

- 3 November: Ukrainian military intelligence said that a car belonging to Igor Kuznetsov, the CEO of the Russian arms manufacturer [GosNIImash](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=GosNIImash\&action=edit\&redlink=1), was set on fire in [Nizhniy Novgorod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nizhniy_Novgorod) by members of the "resistance movement".

  [^cite_note-173]

- 11 November: The governor of Belgorod Oblast said that three houses, power lines and five railway carriages were damaged in a Ukrainian drone and missile strike on [Valuyki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valuyki,_Belgorod_Oblast).

  [^cite_note-174]

  &#x20;A freight train was derailed in [Rybnoye, Ryazan Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rybnoye,_Ryazan_Oblast), which officials blamed on an improvised explosive device.

  [^cite_note-175]

  [^cite_note-176]

- 14 November: A munitions factory in [Seltso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seltso), Bryansk Oblast was reportedly damaged by a Ukrainian drone, while other drones were shot down in Moscow, [Tambov](480933.md), and Oryol Oblasts.

  [^cite_note-177]

- 26 November: Following a Russian drone wave attack on Kyiv, Russian forces downed 24 Ukrainian drones over [Moscow Oblast](372315.md), [Tula Oblast](480930.md), [Kaluga Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaluga_Oblast), [Smolensk Oblast](480938.md) and [Bryansk Oblast](476473.md). One civilian was injured in Tula when a downed drone struck an apartment building. Flights from major Moscow airports were halted.

  [^cite_note-178]

  &#x20;A "booster" from a Russian [Pantsir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pantsir) missile system smashed into a wall of a two-story home in Moscow.

  [^cite_note-179]

- 29 November: a warehouse storing [Shahed drones](75153890.md) in Bryansk was reportedly attacked by Ukrainian drones.

  [^cite_note-180]

- 30 November: Ukrainian media reported that the SBU was behind a series of explosions targeting a freight train at the [Severomuysky Tunnel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Severomuysky_Tunnel), Russia's longest railway tunnel, in the far eastern republic of [Buryatia](741441.md). Separately, Ukrainian military intelligence claimed responsibility for a series of sabotage attacks on railway infrastructure in Moscow Oblast.

  [^cite_note-181]

### December 2023

Main article: [30 December 2023 Belgorod shelling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/30_December_2023_Belgorod_shelling)

On 30 December 2023, the city of [Belgorod](478861.md) [was shelled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/30_December_2023_Belgorod_shelling) allegedly by the [Armed Forces of Ukraine](42799055.md), killing at least 24 people and wounding over 100.&#x20;

### January 2024

- 1 January 2024: there were explosions reported in Belgorod once again. On 2 January, Belgorod was reportedly shelled several times.

  [^cite_note-182]

  [^cite_note-183]

  [^cite_note-184]

  &#x20;A Russian bomber dropped a bomb onto [Petropavlovka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petropavlovka,_Petropavlovsky_District,_Voronezh_Oblast), [Voronezh Oblast](473401.md) due to an "emergency" and caused widespread damage.

  [^cite_note-185]

  [^cite_note-186]

- 3 January, a Russian [Su-34](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Su-34) fighter-bomber was set on fire at [Chelyabinsk Shagol Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chelyabinsk_Shagol_Airport). Ukraine's [Main Directorate of Intelligence](1406269.md) (HUR) said a Ukrainian saboteur was responsible.

  [^cite_note-187]

- 5 January: The HUR said that it had launched a cross-border raid into the [Grayvoronsky District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grayvoronsky_District) of Belgorod Oblast in Russia, mining a road and inflicting casualties on a Russian platoon.

  [^cite_note-188]

In January 2024, Ukrainian drone strikes began targeting [oil and gas terminals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_policy_of_Russia) in Russia. Ukrainian journalist [Illia Ponomarenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illia_Ponomarenko) said that "Russia finances its military from oil exports. You can't persuade countries like India and China to stop buying it. So you knock out Russian oil refineries."[^cite_note-189]&#x20;

- 9 January: A fuel depot and another energy facility in [Oryol Oblast](480945.md) were reportedly attacked by Ukrainian drones, injuring three people.

  [^cite_note-190]

  &#x20;A woman was claimed killed in another attack on the border village of [Gornal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gornal,_Russia) in Kursk Oblast.

  [^cite_note-191]

- 18 January: A Ukrainian drone was shot down over [Vasilevsky Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vasilevsky_Island) in [Saint Petersburg](24320051.md); the first aerial attack on the city since the invasion began. The drone crashed into St Petersburg Oil Terminal. Reportedly, three kilograms of high explosives detonated, burning an area of some 130 square metres. A drone also flew over one of Putin's official residences in [Valday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Valdayskoye), [Novgorod Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novgorod_Oblast), while another was shot down over Moscow. A source from Ukraine's security service acknowledged responsibility.

  [^cite_note-192]

  [^cite_note-193]

- 19 January: An [oil depot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klintsy_oil_depot_attack) in [Klintsy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klintsy), [Bryansk Oblast](476473.md), was struck in a Ukrainian drone strike,

  [^cite_note-194]

  [^cite_note-195]

  [^cite_note-196]

  [^cite_note-197]

  &#x20;starting a fire covering an area of some 1,000 square metres.

  [^cite_note-198]

  [^cite_note-199]

  &#x20;The authorities in Bryansk reported no casualties at the time and the Bryansk governor claimed the drone was intercepted near the town of [Klintsy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klintsy) and its explosives then fell on the oil depot. Another drone was reported to have struck a gunpowder factory in [Tambov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tambov).

  [^cite_note-200]

  [^cite_note-201]

- 21 January: There was a Ukrainian drone strike on the [Novatek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novatek) gas terminal at [Ust-Luga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ust-Luga), [Leningrad Oblast](480845.md). Russian authorities said two storage facilities and a pumping station were damaged.

  [^cite_note-202]

  &#x20;Drone strikes were also reported at the Shcheglovskiy Val plant in [Tula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tula,_Russia), which produces [Pantsir missile systems](8645085.md), the [Smolensk Aviation Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smolensk_Aviation_Plant), and in Oryol Oblast.

  [^cite_note-203]

- 25 January: Fires were reported at [Tuapse oil terminal](42266406.md), [Krasnodar Krai](474125.md). Locals reported several drones in the air at the time.

  [^cite_note-204]

- 31 January: A drone crashed into the grounds of the Nevsky Mazut oil refinery in St Petersburg, causing an explosion and fire.

  [^cite_note-205]

  &#x20;Russian media blamed the blast on a [S-400 missile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S-400_missile) that was fired at a drone but lost control, crashing into the refinery. All flights from St Petersburg's [Pulkovo Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulkovo_Airport) were suspended.

  [^cite_note-206]

### February 2024



Volgograd oil refinery

- 3 February: [Volgograd refinery](77370372.md) in [Volgograd Oblast](478748.md), operated by [Lukoil](1027881.md), was set on fire in a drone attack. The regional governor said a drone crashed into the refinery after being shot down or jammed.

  [^cite_note-207]

- 9 February: Russia claimed to have shot down 19 drones over the Black Sea, Krasnodar Krai and in Kursk, Bryansk, and Oryol Oblasts. One of the drones was reported to have started a fire within the premises of the Ilsky oil refinery in Krasnodar Krai.

  [^cite_note-208]

  [^cite_note-209]

- 15 February: Russian authorities claimed that Ukraine [fired several missiles at Belgorod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/February_2024_Belgorod_missile_strike). They said that fourteen missiles were shot down but one hit and "heavily damaged" a shopping centre in Belgorod city, killing seven people.

  [^cite_note-210]

  [^cite_note-211]

  &#x20;Russia claimed the missiles were fired from an [RM-70 multiple rocket launcher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RM-70_multiple_rocket_launcher).

  [^cite_note-212]

  &#x20;An oil refinery in Kursk Oblast was set on fire by a Ukrainian drone strike, according to local officials.

  [^cite_note-213]

- 23 February: The Ukrainian military shot down a Russian [Beriev A-50](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beriev_A-50) [early warning and control aircraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airborne_early_warning_and_control) over [Krasnodar Krai](474125.md), Russia. It was hit over [Yeysk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yeysk) and crashed in the [Kanevskoy District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanevskoy_District), at least 120 miles from the front line.

  [^cite_note-214]

  [^cite_note-215]

  &#x20;Ukrainian sources reported that ten Russian airmen were killed.

  [^cite_note-216]

- 24 February: The Ukrainian military claimed that its drones set fire to the [Novolipetsk Steel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novolipetsk_Steel) plant in [Lipetsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lipetsk).

  [^cite_note-217]

- 26 February: The Governor of Belgorod Oblast claimed that three people were killed when a Ukrainian drone struck a car in the village of [Pochaevo](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Pochaevo\&action=edit\&redlink=1).

  [^cite_note-218]

- 29 February: The HUR said that a Russian [Pantsir S1](8645085.md) anti-aircraft missile system was damaged in an attack in [Golovchino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golovchino), Belgorod Oblast.

  [^cite_note-219]

### March 2024
