You can help by [adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Attacks_in_Russia_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war_\(2022%E2%80%93present\)\&action=edit\&section=)[adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Attacks_in_Russia_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war_\(2022%E2%80%93present\)\&action=edit\&section=) or [making an edit request](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Talk%3AAttacks_in_Russia_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war_%282022%E2%80%93present%29\&preload=Template%3ASubmit+an+edit+request%2Fpreload\&action=edit\&section=new\&editintro=Template%3AEdit+extended-protected%2Feditintro\&preloadtitle=Extended-confirmed-protected+edit+request+on+3+October+2026\&preloadparams%5B%5D=edit+extended-protected\&preloadparams%5B%5D=Attacks+in+Russia+during+the+Russo-Ukrainian+war+%282022%E2%80%93present%29). *(May 2024)* |
| --- | --- |

- 2 March: A drone crashed into a five-storey apartment block in St Petersburg. Residents were evacuated and there were no casualties. Russian media outlets reported that it may have been a Ukrainian drone, shot down as it headed towards a nearby fuel depot.

  [^cite_note-220]

- 4 March: The HUR claimed responsibility for blowing up a railway bridge in Russia's [Samara Oblast](481962.md), which was used to transport military cargo.

  [^cite_note-221]

- 5 March: The HUR claimed responsibility for a drone strike on an oil depot in the [Gubkinsky District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gubkinsky_District) of Belgorod Oblast.

  [^cite_note-222]

- 6 March: There were drone strikes on Mikhailovsky Mining and Processing Plant and a warehouse in [Zheleznogorsky District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zheleznogorsky_District) of Kursk Oblast. "Several Ukrainian media outlets wrote, citing their sources, that the attack on the Mikhailovsky plant was an operation of Ukrainian intelligence services".

  [^cite_note-223]

- 8 March: Two people were reportedly killed and a third wounded during Ukrainian drone attacks on [Rozhdestvenka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rozhdestvenka,_Belgorod_Oblast), Belgorod Oblast, which came after the Russian Defense Ministry announced it downed several drones over the region.

  [^cite_note-224]

- 9 March: Russia claimed to have downed some 47 Ukrainian drones, mostly over [Rostov Oblast](478744.md), as well as over Volgograd, Belgorod and Kursk Oblasts. The [Beriev Aircraft Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beriev_Aircraft_Company) in [Taganrog](1288065.md) was damaged in the strikes.

  [^cite_note-225]

- 10 March: A woman was killed and her husband wounded by Ukrainian shelling of Kulbaki, according to the regional governor,

  [^cite_note-226]

  &#x20;who also said that a fire broke out at an oil depot following the downing of a drone.

  [^cite_note-227]

  &#x20;Another drone was shot down in Leningrad Oblast.

  [^cite_note-228]

- 11 March: A drone struck the administration building in Belgorod city,

  [^cite_note-229]

  &#x20;another caused a power outage in Belgorod Oblast, while Russian authorities said that drone strikes caused fires at oil depots in [Nizhny Novgorod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nizhny_Novgorod_Oblast) and [Oryol Oblasts](480945.md).

  [^cite_note-230]

  &#x20;Several other drones were reportedly shot down over Moscow, Leningrad, Bryansk, Kursk, Tula and Voronezh Oblasts.

  [^cite_note-231]

- 13 March: Russian forces claimed to have shot down some 60 drones over the country. Drones set fire to [Rosneft](1313566.md)'s [Ryazan refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryazan_refinery) in [Ryazan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryazan), the seventh largest in Russia. Another drone was shot down over the [Kirishi-2 Oil Refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirishi-2_Oil_Refinery), Russia's second largest. Thirty drones were shot down over Voronezh Oblast, causing "minor damage". In Belgorod Oblast, drone wreckage cut power and a gas pipeline.

  [^cite_note-232]

### April 2024

- 2 April: Ukrainian drones struck industrial facilities in the Russian republic of [Tatarstan](67135.md), more than 1,300 km (807 miles) from the Ukrainian border. One hit the [Yelabuga drone factory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yelabuga_drone_factory), causing "significant damage" according to Ukrainian officials.

  [^cite_note-233]

  &#x20;A local Russian official denied any damage to the factory. Russian media reported a nearby workers' dormitory was damaged, injuring twelve workers.

  [^cite_note-234]

  &#x20;Another drone struck the [Tatneft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tatneft) oil refinery in [Nizhnekamsk](2177679.md), causing a fire. The strikes were carried out with light aircraft converted into [kamikaze drones](53703349.md).

  [^cite_note-235]

- 5 April: Ukraine launched a barrage of drones into western Russia, targeting four air bases. Ukrainian officials said at least six military aircraft were destroyed, eight others were "heavily damaged",

  [^cite_note-Fenbert-236]

  [^cite_note-Novikov-237]

  &#x20;and at least 20 personnel were killed or injured at [a military air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morozovsk_air_base) near [Morozovsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morozovsk), [Rostov Oblast](478744.md).

  [^cite_note-Novikov-237]

  [^cite_note-Greenall-238]

  &#x20;Security sources reported the air base held [Su-27](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Su-27) and [Su-34](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Su-34) aircraft,

  [^cite_note-Greenall-238]

  &#x20;while an [open-source intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open-source_intelligence) researcher reported the base also held three [Su-35](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Su-35) aircraft.

  [^cite_note-Zakharchenko-239]

  &#x20;Rostov Oblast Governor [Vasily Golubev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vasily_Golubev_\(politician\)) claimed Russia downed 44 Ukrainian drones and that only a 16-storey residential block

  [^cite_note-Greenall-238]

  &#x20;and power substation were damaged.

  [^cite_note-Fenbert-236]

  [^cite_note-Novikov-237]

  &#x20;Eight people were later injured by one of the fallen drone's explosives detonating during an investigation of the site.

  [^cite_note-Greenall-238]

  &#x20;Drones were also launched at [Engels-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Engels-2_\(air_base\)) air base in [Saratov Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saratov_Oblast), reportedly home to [Tu-95](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tu-95) and [Tu-22](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tu-22) bombers. The attack allegedly caused "serious damage" to three Tu-95MS bombers and killed seven people.

  [^cite_note-Dmytriieva-240]

  &#x20;[Yeysk Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yeysk_Airport), which hosts the aviation units of the [4th Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4th_Guards_Air_and_Air_Defence_Forces_Army) of the [Russian Air Force](1526308.md), was also allegedly struck, with two [Su-25](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Su-25) aircraft destroyed.

  [^cite_note-Dmytriieva-240]

  &#x20;Local officials denied any damage.

  [^cite_note-Zakharchenko-239]

  &#x20;The [Institute for the Study of War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_for_the_Study_of_War) found no visual evidence of Russian aircraft being hit at the air bases.

  [^cite_note-241]

- 7 April: Ukraine's HUR said its operatives set fire to a Russian missile ship, the [Buyan-class corvette](20030984.md) *Serpukhov*, while it was docked in [Kaliningrad Oblast](17311.md). It was the first such attack on the Russian Navy in the [Baltic Sea](3335.md) during the war.

  [^cite_note-242]

  [^cite_note-243]

- 9 April: Russian forces claimed to have intercepted a [Neptune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neptune-MD) missile near the Crimean coast.

  [^cite_note-244]

- 9–10 April: The Russian governor of [Bryansk Oblast](476473.md) said two people were killed by Ukrainian shelling in [Klimovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klimovo,_Bryansk_Oblast),

  [^cite_note-245]

  &#x20;while the governor of Kursk Oblast said three people (including two children) were killed in a Ukrainian drone strike on a car in [Korenevsky District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korenevsky_District).

  [^cite_note-246]

- 16 April: The SBU said it destroyed a Russian [Nebo-U](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nebo-SVU) radar system in a drone strike in [Bryansk Oblast](476473.md).

  [^cite_note-247]

- 17 April: Ukrainian media reported that the HUR launched drone strikes on a radar system in the Russian republic of [Mordovia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mordovia) and a factory making bomber aircraft in [Tatarstan](67135.md).

  [^cite_note-248]

  [^cite_note-249]

- 19 April: A Russian [Tu-22M3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tu-22M3) long range strategic bomber crashed in [Stavropol Krai](474074.md) while returning to base, killing one crew member, with another missing. Ukraine's HUR claimed to have shot it down, at a range of 308 km, with an [S-200 missile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S-200_missile). Russian authorities claimed the aircraft crashed due to a technical malfunction.

  [^cite_note-250]

  [^cite_note-251]

- 26 April: Ukrainian media reported that the HUR carried out an operation that destroyed a Russian [Ka-32](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ka-32) helicopter in [Ostafyevo Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ostafyevo_International_Business_Airport), Moscow.

  [^cite_note-252]

- 27 April: Ukraine launched a drone wave attack in Russia's Krasnodar Krai, starting fires at the [Ilsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilsky) and [Slavyansk-on-Kuban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavyansk-on-Kuban) oil refineries. The [Kushchyovskaya air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kushchyovskaya_\(air_base\)) was also attacked. A number of KAB glide bomb kits were reportedly destroyed along with possibly one Su-34.

  [^cite_note-253]

  [^cite_note-254]

  [^cite_note-255]

  [^cite_note-256]

  [^cite_note-257]

  [^cite_note-258]

  &#x20;The HUR also claimed to have hit a Russian [Podlet-K1 military radar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Podlet-1K_Radar).

  [^cite_note-259]

### May 2024

- 6 May: The governor of Belgorod Oblast said that eight factory workers were killed and 35 others were wounded in a Ukrainian drone strike that hit three vehicles in the village of [Berezovka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berezovka,_Borisovsky_District,_Belgorod_Oblast).

  [^cite_note-260]

  [^cite_note-261]

- 9 May: The governor of Belgorod Oblast said that eight people were injured in a Ukrainian airstrike in [Belgorod](478861.md) city.

  [^cite_note-262]

  &#x20;A drone attack on [Krasnodar](474128.md) also set a fuel depot on fire.

  [^cite_note-263]

  &#x20;Another drone struck an oil refinery in [Salavat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salavat,_Russia); the first such attack in the Russian republic of [Bashkortostan](735530.md).

  [^cite_note-264]

  &#x20;The drone was a light aircraft that travelled some 1,500 km.

  [^cite_note-265]

  [^cite_note-266]

- 11 May: Russian officials said that five people were killed and nine injured by drone strikes in Belgorod and Kursk oblasts.

  [^cite_note-267]

- 12 May: Russian officials said that [16 people were killed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/May_2024_Belgorod_missile_strike) and 27 others were injured when wreckage of a downed [Tochka-U](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tochka-U) missile struck an apartment building in Belgorod.

  [^cite_note-268]

  [^cite_note-269]

  &#x20;However, independent military experts considered this version unlikely, noting that the building was hit on its northeastern side, which is not facing Ukrainian territory. They said that the origin of the explosion was probably a Russian surface-to-air missile, or a Russian guided aerial bomb dropped accidentally.

  [^cite_note-270]

  [^cite_note-271]

  [^cite_note-272]

- 14 May: Russian state media reported that a drone strike derailed a freight train at [Kotluban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kotluban) station in Volgograd Oblast. A storage tank carrying diesel caught fire, another exploded and nine rail cars were derailed. No injuries were reported.

  [^cite_note-273]

  [^cite_note-274]

- 23 May: The long-range [early nuclear warning system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early-warning_radar) [Voronezh-DM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voronezh_radar) in [Armavir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armavir_Radar_Station), southern Russia, was damaged by Ukrainian drone attack.

  [^cite_note-275]

- 26 May: A Ukrainian drone attacked the Voronezh M long-range radar station near [Orsk](1616356.md) in Russia's [Orenburg Oblast](481957.md) near the Kazakhstan–Russia border.

  [^cite_note-276]

- 31 May: Ukraine launched [Neptune missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neptune-MD) and drones at a ferry crossing and an oil depot in [Port Kavkaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Kavkaz), Krasnodar Krai. Various sources reported three petroleum storage tanks were damaged and a fire broke out. Additional facilities at the Kavkaz port were damaged, including a train. A power substation connected to the Crimean Bridge was also damaged.

  [^cite_note-277]

  [^cite_note-278]

  &#x20;Russian authorities claimed that two people were injured in the attack on the depot.

  [^cite_note-279]

### June 2024

- 1 June: Ukraine reportedly fired [HIMARS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HIMARS) rockets into Russia for the first time. Russian officials said that 10 HIMARS rockets were shot down over Belgorod Oblast.

  [^cite_note-280]

  &#x20;On 3 June, a Russian S-300/400 missile system was destroyed by HIMARS rockets in Belgorod.

  [^cite_note-281]

- 6 June: there were several explosions and a fire at the Novoshakhtinsk oil refinery, which the governor of Rostov Oblast claimed was caused by a drone attack.

  [^cite_note-282]

- 8 June: The HUR claimed to have damaged two Russian [Su-57](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Su-57) fighter jets for the first time using drones, during a strike on the [Akhtubinsk air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akhtubinsk_\(air_base\)) in Astrakhan Oblast.

  [^cite_note-283]

  &#x20;Imagery released by the HUR as well as a Telegram channel close to the Russian Aerospace Forces confirmed that one Su-57 was damaged.

  [^cite_note-284]

  &#x20;Russia claimed to have shot down three Ukrainian drones targeting an air base near [Mozdok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mozdok), in the first such attack in [North Ossetia-Alania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Ossetia-Alania) since the war began.

  [^cite_note-285]

- 14 June: The HUR said that two Russian [Su-34s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Su-34) were damaged in a Ukrainian drone strike on the [Morozovsk air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morozovsk_\(air_base\)) in Rostov Oblast, with unconfirmed reports of Russian six pilots killed and 10 soldiers wounded.

  [^cite_note-286]

  &#x20;Russian officials also said that drones struck power plants in Rostov and Voronezh Oblasts.

  [^cite_note-287]

  [^cite_note-288]

- 15 June: the governor of Belgorod Oblast claimed that five people were killed by Ukrainian shelling in [Shebekino](4570394.md).

  [^cite_note-289]

- 17 June: Ukraine launched a drone attack on western Russia, with the HUR claiming to have struck a metallurgical plant and military production facilities in Belgorod, Voronezh, and Lipetsk Oblasts.

  [^cite_note-290]

- 20 June: Russian media reported an attack by Ukrainian drones on the [Afipsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afipsky) oil refinery in Krasnodar Krai,

  [^cite_note-291]

  &#x20;as well as in oil depots in [Tambov Oblast](480933.md) and [Enem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enem_\(urban-type_settlement\)), [Adygea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adygea). Authorities also claimed that drones were shot down over Bryansk, Rostov, Belgorod and Oryol Oblasts.

  [^cite_note-292]

  &#x20;One person was killed in a drone strike in [Slavyansk-on-Kuban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavyansk-on-Kuban).

  [^cite_note-293]

- 21 June: Russia said that Ukraine launched over 100 drones at Crimea, Krasnodar Krai, and Volgograd Oblast. Russia's Defense Ministry said that 43 drones were shot down over Krasnodar Krai, a further 70 over Crimea and one in Volgograd. Russian officials claimed one person in Krasnodar Krai was killed and six injured. Reports suggested that a fire broke out at the Ilya refinery in Krasnodar Krai. The [Yeysk air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yeysk_air_base), also in Krasnodar Krai, was attacked.

  [^cite_note-294]

  &#x20;Ukraine said that a [Neptune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neptune-MD) missile was used to strike the Shahed drone depot and training facility in Krasnodar.

  [^cite_note-295]

- 25 June: The HUR claimed to have blown up a Russian ammunition depot in Voronezh Oblast.

  [^cite_note-296]

  &#x20;The governor of Belgorod Oblast claimed that one person was killed in a drone strike.

  [^cite_note-297]

- 28 June: the governor of Tambov Oblast said that an oil depot was set on fire by a drone strike.

  [^cite_note-298]

- 29 June: Russian officials said that five people, including two children, were killed in a Ukrainian drone strike in [Gorodishche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gorodishche,_Kursky_District,_Kursk_Oblast), Kursk Oblast.

  [^cite_note-299]

### July 2024

- 9 July: Russia claimed to have shot down 38 drones over Belgorod, Voronezh, Astrakhan, Kursk, and Rostov oblasts. An oil depot was set on fire in [Kalach-na-Donu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalach-na-Donu), Volgograd Oblast, along with two power substations. Russian sources claimed one killed and two injured in Belgorod Oblast.

  [^cite_note-300]

  [^cite_note-301]

  &#x20;Ukrainian drones also struck a Russian missile testing facility at [Kapustin Yar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kapustin_Yar).

  [^cite_note-302]

  [^cite_note-303]

- 13 July: the governor of Rostov Oblast said an oil depot was set on fire by a drone strike in [Tsimlyansky District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsimlyansky_District).

  [^cite_note-304]

- 17 July: the governor of Belgorod Oblast said that two people were killed in a drone strike on the border village of [Tserkovny](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Tserkovny\&action=edit\&redlink=1).

  [^cite_note-305]

- 18 July: The [Freedom of Russia Legion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_Russia_Legion) claimed to have launched a sabotage attack on [Bolshoye Savino airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perm_International_Airport) in [Perm](389777.md), which hosts [MiG-31](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MiG-31) fighters. A video showed two [KAMAZ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KAMAZ) trucks burning, with the group claiming to have destroyed "several units of military equipment".

  [^cite_note-306]

- 20 July: Sixteen explosions and several fires were reported near [Millerovo air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millerovo_\(air_base\)) in Rostov Oblast, following a drone strike.

  [^cite_note-307]

- 21 July: The HUR said it carried out sabotage at the [Russian Helicopters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Helicopters) facility in Moscow, damaging three helicopters: an Mi-8, an Mi-28a, and a Ka-226.

  [^cite_note-308]

- 23 July: the governor of Krasnodar Krai said that a Ukrainian drone attack on a ferry in [Port Kavkaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Kavkaz) killed one person and started a fire on board.

  [^cite_note-309]

- 24 July: A [GRU](261749.md) officer and his wife were wounded by a bomb planted under their car in Moscow.

  [^cite_note-310]

- 25 July: the governor of Belgorod Oblast said one person was killed and two others were injured by Ukrainian shelling in [Shebekino](4570394.md).

  [^cite_note-311]

- 27 July: Ukrainian drones reportedly struck the [Olenya air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olenya_\(air_base\)) in [Murmansk Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murmansk_Oblast), some 1,800 kilometers from the Ukrainian border, damaging two Tu-22M3s. Engels and Dyagilevo airfields were also attacked, along with an oil refinery in Ryazan.

  [^cite_note-312]

- 28 July: A Ukrainian drone attack struck an oil deport in [Polevaya, Kursk Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polevaya,_Kursk_Oblast) causing a multi-day fire

  [^cite_note-313]

  &#x20;detected by [NASA](18426568.md)'s [FIRMS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fire_Information_for_Resource_Management_System).

- 31 July: Russian forces claimed to have intercepted a [Neptune missile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neptune_missile) and 19 drones over Belgorod, Bryansk, Kursk, Kaluga, and Rostov oblasts. The acting governor of Kursk, [Alexei Smirnov](77095785.md), said that a facility caught fire after midnight. Ukraine said it was a "storage facility for weapons and military equipment". Fires at two military facilities south of [Kursk Vostochny Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kursk_Vostochny_Airport) were detected by [NASA](18426568.md)'s [FIRMS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fire_Information_for_Resource_Management_System).

  [^cite_note-314]

  [^cite_note-315]

  [^cite_note-316]

### August 2024

- 3 August: A major drone attack was made on [Morozovsk air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morozovsk_air_base) with locals posting multiple large explosions, including secondary explosions.

  [^cite_note-317]

  [^cite_note-318]

- 8 August: The [Lipetsk air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lipetsk_air_base) was struck by a large-scale Ukrainian drone attack that caused numerous explosions and fires and reportedly injured six people.

  [^cite_note-319]

  [^cite_note-320]

  [^cite_note-321]

- 14 August: One of the most numerous UAV attacks on Russian territory was carried out, with 117 drones being used. Of these, 11 drones hit the [Savasleyka air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Savasleyka_air_base) in the [Nizhny Novgorod Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nizhny_Novgorod_Oblast), which became the most numerous attack since the beginning of hostilities.

  [^cite_note-322]

  &#x20;[Governor of Nizhny Novgorod Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_Nizhny_Novgorod_Oblast) [Gleb Nikitin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gleb_Nikitin) said that all targets were shot down by the air defense system, there were no destructions or losses.

  [^cite_note-323]

- 18 August: An attack on an oil depot in [Proletarsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proletarsk), approximately 250 kilometers from the Ukrainian border, resulted in a massive multi-day fire at the extensive [petroleum](23195.md) tank farm.

  [^cite_note-324]

  [^cite_note-325]

  &#x20;The tank farm was still burning on 23 August 2024,

  [^cite_note-326]

  [^cite_note-327]

  &#x20;while Ukrainian Armed Forces struck it again on that day.

  [^cite_note-328]

  [^cite_note-329]

- 21 August: Russia announced it had intercepted 11 Ukrainian attack drones heading towards Moscow.

  [^cite_note-330]

- 22 August: [Marinovka air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marinovka_air_base) was attacked by Ukrainian drones, resulting in explosions and a fire at the air base.

  [^cite_note-331]

  [^cite_note-332]

- 26 August: During an attack on the [Omsk refinery](77362435.md), an explosion and subsequent fire

  [^cite_note-333]

  &#x20;destroyed the crude distillation unit CDU-11, one of the "two largest primary refining units at the plant" responsible for roughly one-third of plant capacity.

  [^cite_note-334]

- 28 August: The Atlas oil depot, located in the Kamenski District of [Rostov Oblast](478744.md), was [attacked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlas_oil_depot_attack). This depot is a division of the [Federal Agency for State Reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Agency_for_State_Reserves) in the Southern Federal District, specializing in supplying petroleum products to the [Russian Armed Forces](25709.md).

  [^cite_note-335]

  [^cite_note-336]

  &#x20;A second drone attack on the depot occurred late in the day, renewing the "powerful blaze".

  [^cite_note-ki20240829-337]

- 28 August: The Zenit oil depot, in [Kotelnich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kotelnich), [Kirov Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirov_Oblast), was [attacked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zenit_oil_depot_attack). This facility is located more than 1,200 kilometers away from the Ukrainian border.

  [^cite_note-ki20240829-337]

  [^cite_note-338]

- 31 August: [Belgorod Oblast](478857.md) governor [Vyacheslav Gladkov](67064358.md) claimed that five civilians were killed in [Ukrainian](42799055.md) missile strikes on [Belgorod](478861.md).

  [^cite_note-339]

### September 2024



NASA [FIRMS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fire_Information_for_Resource_Management_System) imagery from 21 September showing the extensive fire near Oktyabrsky with first detections at 00\:18\:00 ([UTC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UTC)) and the still ongoing fire from the [attack east of Toropets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toropets_depot_explosions) three days before

- 1 September: Ukraine launched over a hundred drones against targets in Russia, including the [Konakovo Power Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konakovo_Power_Station) in [Konakovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konakovo), [Tver Oblast](463952.md) and the [Moscow Refinery](77379611.md) in [Moscow](19004.md).

  [^cite_note-340]

- 10 September: Ukraine launched 144 drones against Moscow in the largest drone attack against Russia in the war, with Russian authorities reporting dozens of homes being damaged and two people killed by the attack.

  [^cite_note-341]

  [^cite_note-342]

- 18 September: In the early morning, Ukraine launched [a successful drone attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toropets_depot_explosions) on the [Main Missile and Artillery Directorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Main_Missile_and_Artillery_Directorate) (GRAU) 107th Ammunition Depot in [Toropets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toropets), causing a massive series of explosions and fires which damaged much of the town.

  [^cite_note-Lukiv-343]

  [^cite_note-344]

  &#x20;The attack resulted in an earthquake-magnitude blast, and NASA [FIRMS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fire_Information_for_Resource_Management_System) satellites detected the resulting fires over an area of approximately 13 km

  2

  &#x20;(5 sq mi).

  [^cite_note-Cole-345]

  &#x20;The blast wave spread up to 200 mi (320 km) and was estimated to be consistent with 200–240 tonnes of TNT (840–1,000 GJ) of high-explosives detonating.

  [^cite_note-Cole-345]

  [^cite_note-346]

  [^cite_note-Smith-347]

  &#x20;The [Security Service of Ukraine](709222.md) claimed that "[Iskander](5607520.md), [Tochka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OTR-21_Tochka) and KAB missiles" were stored at the facility.

  [^cite_note-Smith-347]

  &#x20;Russian officials reported that 13 people had been injured and that an evacuation of the area had been ordered.

  [^cite_note-Lukiv-343]

  &#x20;The [Estonian Defense Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estonian_Defense_Forces) Intelligence Center subsequently estimated that the strike had caused 30,000 tonnes of munitions to explode.

  [^cite_note-348]

- 21 September: On the night of 20–21 September 2024, Ukraine launched [drone attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toropets_depot_explosions) on two Russian ammunition depots—the 23rd Arsenal of the GRAU near the village of [Oktyabrsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Oktyabrsky,_Tver_Oblast\&action=edit\&redlink=1) in [Tver Oblast](463952.md) and the 719th Artillery Ammunition Base near [Tikhoretsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tikhoretsk) and the [Tikhoretsk air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tikhoretsk_\(air_base\)) in [Krasnodar Krai](474125.md).

  [^cite_note-349]

### October 2024

- 3 October: Ukrainian drones struck the [Borisoglebsk air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borisoglebsk_air_base) in the Voronezh Oblast, targeting Su-34s, Su-35s, fuel tanks and a warehouse for Russian glide bombs.

  [^cite_note-350]

- 10 October: A Ukrainian drone attack targeted the [Khanskaya air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khanskaya_\(air_base\)) near [Maykop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maykop) causing a fire detected by [NASA](18426568.md)'s [FIRMS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fire_Information_for_Resource_Management_System).

  [^cite_note-351]

### November 2024

- 6 November: An [A-22](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aeroprakt_A-22_Foxbat) light aircraft converted into a drone struck the Kaspiysk [naval base](383258.md), reportedly damaging two [Gepard-class frigate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gepard-class_frigate) s and one [Buyan-class corvette](20030984.md) of the [Caspian Flotilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caspian_Flotilla).

  [^cite_note-352]

  [^cite_note-353]

- 8–9 November: Ukrainian drones attacked the Aleksin Chemical Plant—part of the [state corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_corporation) [Rostec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rostec)—in the [Aleksinsky District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleksinsky_District), about 190 km (120 mi) south of Moscow, causing a series of explosions and fires at the plant which manufactures ammunition and explosives for the Russian military. Russian sources did not acknowledge the attack's success, "as is common following a reported attack by Ukraine on one of \[Russia's] military sites."

  [^cite_note-354]

  [^cite_note-355]

- 10 November: [At least 34 Ukrainian drones attacked Moscow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/November_2024_Moscow_drone_attack).

  [^cite_note-356]

- 14 November: Ukrainian drones attacked the [Krymsk air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krymsk_air_base) in [Krasnodar Krai](474125.md), where [Su-27](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Su-27) and [Su-30](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Su-30) fighter jets and [Ka-27](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ka-27) helicopters are stationed.

  [^cite_note-357]

  &#x20;The Russian MOD said they shot down 51 drones using conventional air defense capabilities and that no casualties were reported.

  [^cite_note-358]

- 17 November: The [Center for Countering Disinformation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_Countering_Disinformation) reported that several drones struck the [Izhevsk Electromechanical Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Izhevsk_Electromechanical_Plant).

  [^cite_note-359]

- 20 November: Ukrainian drones struck the 13th Arsenal of the [Main Missile and Artillery Directorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Main_Missile_and_Artillery_Directorate) located in [Kotovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kotovo#Novgorod_Oblast), [Novgorod Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novgorod_Oblast), some 680 kilometres from the Ukrainian border. Residents of Kotovo were evacuated to nearby [Okulovka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Okulovka_\(town\),_Novgorod_Oblast) as a precaution, with several drones being reported as being shot down by Russian air defences.

  [^cite_note-360]

- 22 November 2024: Multiple [Storm Shadow](306908.md) missiles were used by Ukraine to attack the Maryino estate, located 32 km behind the front lines in Kursk, a Russian army command post and communications hub. According to *[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)*, a [North Korean](21255.md) general officer was wounded in the attack.

  [^cite_note-361]

- 22 & 25 November: The Rosneft Kaluga oil storage depot was attacked again by Ukrainian drones. Russian sources claimed air defences to have shot down two missiles and 27 drones. However, fires continued to burn at the depot on the 24th, and additional drones attacked on the 25th resulting in explosions and fires.

  [^cite_note-362]

  [^cite_note-363]

  &#x20;The 25 November fire was detected by NASA [FIRMS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fire_Information_for_Resource_Management_System). By 28 November, three of the ten Kaluga storage tanks had been completely destroyed in the multi-day fires.

  [^cite_note-364]

### December 2024

- 1 December: According to Russian authorities, Ukrainian drone attacks took place in the Bryansk and Kaluga regions.

  [^cite_note-365]

- 14 December: An oil depot in [Oryol](488242.md) was attacked and set on fire by Ukrainian drones. Russian social media reported explosions and the governor confirmed a fire had broken out at an "infrastructure facility".

  [^cite_note-366]

- 19 December: An oil refinery in [Novoshakhtinsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novoshakhtinsk) was struck by Ukrainian drones and missiles, according to the General Staff of the Armed Forces of Ukraine. The involvement of the Ukrainian Navy and recent attacks near the facility pointed to the use of Neptune missiles.

  [^cite_note-367]

- 21 December: [Kazan International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazan_International_Airport) in [Tatarstan](67135.md) and [Gagarin Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saratov_Gagarin_Airport) in the city of [Saratov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saratov) were temporarily closed due to Ukrainian drone attacks.

  [^cite_note-368]

- 25 December: According to Russian authorities, Ukrainian drones attacked the cities of [Grozny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grozny), [Mozdok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mozdok) and [Vladikavkaz](229915.md) in the [North Caucasus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Caucasus).

  [^cite_note-369]

- 27 December: Airports in [Sochi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sochi), [Kazan](202295.md), [Samara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samara), [Grozny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grozny), [Makhachkala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makhachkala), [Volgograd](32585.md) and [Astrakhan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astrakhan) were temporarily closed due to the threat of drone attacks.

  [^cite_note-370]

- 31 December: Multiple [Storm Shadow](306908.md) long-range missiles struck [Lgov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lgov,_Kursk_Oblast) hitting a Russian command bunker and a Russian troop barracks of the [810th Naval Infantry Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/810th_Naval_Infantry_Brigade). Initial Russian news reports were that 8 military members were killed and 22 injured and hospitalized.

  [^cite_note-371]

### January 2025

| \[icon] | This section **needs expansion** with: with other attacks in Russia during January. You can help by [adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Attacks_in_Russia_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war_\(2022%E2%80%93present\)\&action=edit\&section=)[adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Attacks_in_Russia_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war_\(2022%E2%80%93present\)\&action=edit\&section=) or [making an edit request](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Talk%3AAttacks_in_Russia_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war_%282022%E2%80%93present%29\&preload=Template%3ASubmit+an+edit+request%2Fpreload\&action=edit\&section=new\&editintro=Template%3AEdit+extended-protected%2Feditintro\&preloadtitle=Extended-confirmed-protected+edit+request+on+3+October+2026\&preloadparams%5B%5D=edit+extended-protected\&preloadparams%5B%5D=Attacks+in+Russia+during+the+Russo-Ukrainian+war+%282022%E2%80%93present%29). *(January 2025)* |
| --- | --- |

- 14 January: Drones attacked the [Kazan Orgsintez chemical plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazanorgsintez) in the suburbs of [Kazan, Tatarstan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazan,_Tatarstan), nearly 1,000 km (620 mi) from the border with Ukraine, causing a series of explosions and fires in three [liquified natural gas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liquified_natural_gas) (LNG) storage tanks. Russian sources acknowledged the attack.

  [^cite_note-372]

  [^cite_note-373]

  &#x20;A fuel storage facility supplying the Engels air base was also struck by drones.

  [^cite_note-374]

- 17 January: Ukrainian drones struck an oil depot in [Lyudinovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyudinovo) with falling debris causing a fire.

  [^cite_note-375]

- 20 January: Ukrainian drones attacked the [Gorbunov Kazan Aviation Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazan_Aircraft_Production_Association) in [Kazan, Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazan,_Russia) where the [Tu-160](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tu-160) strategic bomber is built, about 1,000 km (620 mi) from the border with Ukraine, causing several explosions. Local Russian sources stated that damage was minimal and all drone threats had been neutralized.

  [^cite_note-376]

  [^cite_note-377]

- 29 January: [Ukraine](31750.md) launched a drone attack on the [Kstovo refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kstovo_refinery) in [Nizhny Novgorod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nizhny_Novgorod), Russia, about 800 km (500 mi) from the border with Ukraine, causing several explosions and resulting in large fires in several units.

Ukrainian military sources claimed that 4 of the 4 drones sent struck their targets. Local residents reported at least three explosions inside the refinery. Russian officials downplayed the extent of the damage.[^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379][^cite_note-380] Russian authorities subsequently said the blaze burned for two days and Sibur temporarily halted operations at the plant.[^cite_note-381]&#x20;

- 31 January: Ukrainian drones attacked the [Volgograd refinery](77370372.md), causing a fire at the facility. Russian sources said the fire was quickly extinguished.

  [^cite_note-382]

### June 2025

Main article: [Operation Spiderweb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Spiderweb)

- 1 June: SBU struck at least two airfields, those being [Olenya air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olenya_air_base), in the Arctic, and [Belaya air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belaya_air_base) in Siberia, destroying rows of Russian strategic and nuclear capable bombers.

  [^cite_note-383]

  &#x20;Ukrainian sources claimed further strikes at [Diaghilev air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dyagilevo_air_base) and [Ivanovo air base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivanovo_Severny_air_base), claiming to have hit over 40 Russian aircraft including the [A-50](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beriev_A-50), [Tu-95](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tu-95) and [Tu-22](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tu-22)M3.

  [^cite_note-384]

### August 2025

- 7–8 August: An overnight Ukrainian drone strike sparked a fire at the [Afipsky refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afipsky_refinery) in [Krasnodar Krai](474125.md). The regional crisis center reported that drone debris ignited a gas condensate processing unit. The fire was reportedly extinguished by 8\:21 a.m. local time.

  [^cite_note-385]

- 14 August: Russia's military claimed to have downed 9 drones overnight in [Volgograd Oblast](478748.md), while Volgograd's governor claimed that falling drone debris caused a fire at the [Volgograd refinery](77370372.md).

  [^cite_note-386]

  [^cite_note-387]

  &#x20;Extensive fire at the refinery was detected by [NASA](18426568.md)'s [FIRMS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fire_Information_for_Resource_Management_System) on 13 August 2025 21\:46\:00 ([UTC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UTC)).

- 21 August: Ukraine attacked the [Unecha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unecha) oil pumping station in [Bryansk Oblast](476473.md), a critical part of Russia's [Druzhba oil pipeline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druzhba_oil_pipeline) to Europe. Governor of Bryansk Oblast [Alexander Bogomaz](63834484.md) said on 22 August that an energy facility at Unecha had caught fire following Ukrainian missile and drone attacks.

  [^cite_note-388]

  &#x20;As a result, Russian oil supplies to [Hungary](13275.md) and [Slovakia](26830.md) were suspended until 28 August.

  [^cite_note-389]

- 24 August:&#x20;

  - Russia claimed that the [Kursk Nuclear Power Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kursk_Nuclear_Power_Plant) was targeted by a Ukrainian drone strike. The attack caused a fire that was quickly extinguished and damaged an auxiliary transformer, leading to a 50% reduction in the operating capacity of one reactor. No injuries were reported, and radiation levels remained within normal limits according to the [International Atomic Energy Agency](14984.md).

    [^cite_note-390]

  - Governor of [Leningrad Oblast](480845.md) [Aleksandr Drozdenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleksandr_Drozdenko) said that least 10 Ukrainian drones were downed over the port of [Ust-Luga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ust-Luga), on the [Gulf of Finland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_of_Finland), with falling debris sparking a fire at the [Novatek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novatek)-operated terminal.

    [^cite_note-391]

- 28 August: Ukrainian drones struck the [Kuibyshev refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuibyshev_refinery) in [Samara Oblast](481962.md), damaging both of its primary oil refining units as well as secondary units. The refinery had resumed oil refining on 21 August following a major overhaul that started on 1 July 2025.

  [^cite_note-392]

### September 2025

- 20 September: Four people were killed and one person was injured in a Ukrainian drone attack on oil [refineries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refineries) in [Novokuybyshevsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novokuybyshevsk), [Samara Oblast](481962.md) and [Saratov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saratov), [Saratov Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saratov_Oblast), Russia.

  [^cite_note-393]

- 23 September: Four [Flamingo missiles](80778495.md) struck a Belgorod factory belonging to the Skif-M company, which manufactures components for the production of the [Su-34](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Su-34), [Su-35](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Su-35), and [Su-57](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Su-57) aircraft. Initially it was reported by Russia that only one missile had hit, but satellite imagery in January showed that all missiles struck the factory. However, one missile appeared to have penetrated the roof and exited through a wall, exploding outside the factory.

  [^cite_note-394]

  &#x20;The blast crater was approximately 25 metres wide. Both Russia and Zelenskyy confirmed the strike on 9 October, marking the first combat use of Flamingo missiles against targets on Russian soil.

  [^cite_note-395]

- 28 September: A Ukrainian [missile](19594.md) struck a [power station](212141.md) in [Belgorod](478861.md) causing a massive [power outage](296636.md) in the Russian city.

  [^cite_note-396]

- 29 September: Four Neptune missiles, launched by units of the missile and artillery forces of the Armed Forces of Ukraine in cooperation with the Navy, struck the main shop of the Electrodetal plant in [Karachev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karachev).

  [^cite_note-397]

### October 2025

- 4 October: A Russian [Buyan-class corvette](20030984.md) was allegedly attacked near [Lake Onega](374540.md), in [Karelia](199243.md), Russia. The ship sustained damage to the right side of its power plant compartment.

  [^cite_note-398]

- 8 October: A Ukrainian [missile](19594.md) strike kills three people and injures nine others in [Maslova Pristan](59136227.md), [Belgorod Oblast](478857.md), Russia. A [search and rescue](172599.md) operation was done for others that were believed to be trapped under rubble.

  [^cite_note-399]

### December 2025

- 17 December: Ukrainian forces attacked and damaged a Russian submarine with a naval drone.

  [^cite_note-400]

- 20 December: For the first time during the war, Ukrainian forces executed an action on [Caspian sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caspian_sea) against a Russian war ship.

  [^cite_note-401]

- 22 December: Ukraine launched a series of strikes against Russian targets, damaging an oil terminal, a pipeline, two fighter jets, and two ships. Among the sites hit were the Tamanneftegaz oil terminal, an ammunition depot, and a drone launch facility on Russian soil and in occupied Ukrainian areas. In Russia's Krasnodar region, attacks caused a serious fire involving two docks and two ships. A Ukrainian missile also hit a temporary installation of the Russian 92nd Riverine Brigade in Olenivka, Crimea, while an ammunition depot in the Donetsk region was damaged to hinder Russian advances. On Sunday evening, Ukrainian partisans set fire to two Russian jets at an airfield near Lipetsk. Ukrainian drones attacked a Russian airfield in Crimea damaging a radar facility and destroying two planes and several drones. Attacks came shortly after Russia violently attacked Ukraine with drones and missiles.

  [^cite_note-402]

- 29 December: 91 Ukrainian drones flew into Russian territory, with the Russian government claiming that this attack targeted [Vladimir Putin](32817.md)'s residence in the [Novgorod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novgorod) region, which the Ukrainian government rejected. On 31 December, the Russian Ministry of Defense released a video detailing the attack, showing a downed drone, and reporting that the drones had been shot down.

  [^cite_note-403]

### February 2026

- 8 February: the Ukrainian General Staff claimed to have struck the Russian test range at [Kapustin Yar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kapustin_Yar) using FP-5 Flamingo missiles.

  [^cite_note-404]

- 12 February: a Russian [Main Missile and Artillery Directorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Main_Missile_and_Artillery_Directorate) ammunition depot, near Kotluban, Volgograd Oblast, was attacked by Flamingo missiles resulting in a "series of powerful explosions" and precautionary evacuations of local people according to [Governor of Volgograd Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_Volgograd_Oblast) [Andrey Bocharov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrey_Bocharov).

  [^cite_note-405]

  [^cite_note-406]

- 20 February: Ukraine's General Staff stated a strike of Flamingo missiles targeted the [Votkinsk Machine Building Plant](5145252.md) 1,400 km (870 miles) from Ukraine, which produces [Iskander](5607520.md) ballistic missiles.

  [^cite_note-apnews-russia-ukraine-factory-407]

  &#x20;[Udmurt](447145.md) regional head [Alexander Brechalov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Brechalov) said one of the republic's facilities was attacked by drones, and unofficial reports specified the site as the Votkinsk Plant.

  [^cite_note-wp-ukraine-strike-russia-408]

### March 2026

- 2 March: [Ukrainian](42799055.md) drones struck an [oil terminal](3385681.md) in the [Russian](25391.md) port city of [Novorossiysk](1190336.md), [Krasnodar Krai](474125.md). Four residential buildings in the area were reportedly damaged in the attack.

  [^cite_note-409]

- 10 March: Six people were killed and 37 others were injured in a [Ukrainian](42799055.md) missile attack in [Bryansk](476476.md).

  [^cite_note-410]

- 21 March: Two people were killed and another was injured in a [Ukrainian](42799055.md) artillery strike in [Belgorod Oblast](478857.md), Russia.

  [^cite_note-411]

- 25 March: A Ukrainian drone strike killed two people in [Belgorod Oblast](478857.md), Russia, according to [Belgorod governor](68851709.md) [Vyacheslav Gladkov](67064358.md).

- 28 March: Flamingo missiles struck the JSC Promsintez explosives plant in [Chapaevsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chapaevsk), [Samara Oblast](481962.md).

  [^cite_note-412]

  &#x20;Subsequent satellite images showed that the attack "achieved accurate terminal approach but failed to deliver direct structural penetration of the production halls". One missile was shot down "at the last moment", a second struck a lightning rod tower and the third missile detonated close potentially causing "minor peripheral damage".

  [^cite_note-413]

### April 2026

- 16 April: Ukrainian drones [began striking oil infrastructure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Tuapse_environmental_disaster) in [Tuapse](1623160.md).

- 29 April:&#x20;

  - Ukrainian drones [began striking oil infrastructure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Perm_environmental_disaster) in [Perm](389777.md).

  - Three people were killed and eight others injured in a separate Ukrainian drone strike on a passenger bus in [Voznesenovka](61807074.md), [Belgorod Oblast](478857.md), Russia.

    [^cite_note-414]

### May 2026

- 5 May: Zelenskyy tweeted that Ukraine's armed forces had used Flamingos to conduct strikes on targets 1500 km inside Russia, including military-industrial facilities in [Cheboksary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheboksary).

  [^cite_note-415]

  &#x20;The JSC VNIIR-Progress missile plant in Cheboksary was attacked with video on social media showing a large fire. The plant manufactures the Kometa antenna, used in guided missiles and [electronic warfare](82272.md) systems. Russian milbloggers and [OSINT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OSINT) analysts claimed a Flamingo missile was used during the attack. Footage online showed "a large blast followed by sustained fire". Officials said that at least four [Liutyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liutyi) drones followed up the attack.

  [^cite_note-416]

  [^cite_note-417]

- 12 May: Zelensky announced that [the military](42799055.md) had attacked several [gas](42827258.md) facilities in [Orenburg Oblast](481957.md), [Russia](25391.md).

- 17 May: Ukraine launched a massive [drone attack](38212231.md) on [Russia](25391.md), killing at least four people, with strikes reported in [Moscow](372315.md) and [Belgorod Oblast](478857.md), after ‌facing its largest attack in more than a year.

- 23 May: A [Ukrainian](31750.md) drone attack caused a fire at a [Transneft](5707669.md) [oil terminal](3385681.md) in [Novorossiysk](1190336.md), [Krasnodar Krai](474125.md), [Russia](25391.md).

  [^cite_note-418]

- 24 May: The [Ukrainian Security Service](709222.md) reported that [Ukrainian forces](42799055.md) had successfully launched an overnight [drone strike](38212231.md) on the [Vtorovo](61981894.md) oil pumping station in the [Vladimir Oblast](463919.md) in [Russia](25391.md).

  [^cite_note-419]

### June 2026

- 3 June: Ukrainian drones struck an [oil terminal](3385681.md) in [Saint Petersburg](24320051.md), amidst the [economic forum](26267797.md) scheduled to be held in the city and military facilities at a naval base in [Kronstadt](763125.md).

  [^cite_note-420]

- 5 June: Ukrainian drones struck two commercial vessels in [Taganrog Bay](3032433.md), [Rostov Oblast](478744.md). Five [Azerbaijani](746.md) crew members were killed and three others were injured.

  [^cite_note-421]

Fire at the [Moscow Oil Refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moscow_Oil_Refinery) on June 18, 2026

- 10 June: the VNIIR-Progress plant in [Cheboksary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheboksary) came under further Flamingo missile attack. Local people reported a "powerful explosion" after the impact, with a plume of smoke over the building.

  [^cite_note-422]

  [^cite_note-423]

  &#x20;Zelenskyy confirmed the use of Flamingo missiles.

  [^cite_note-KyivPostFlamingo2026-424]

  &#x20;The attack is thought to have been carried out by five missiles, two of which were shot down near [Chuvashia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuvashia) and one near the target at Cheboksary, with two striking the target. Radar and local people detected the missiles at the beginning of their flight path, over several Russian regions, and on their final approach, which passed Cheboksary itself and reached the VNIIR-Progress plant from the east. This could "indicate the use of routes bypassing large population centers and areas with dense radar coverage".

  [^cite_note-425]

- 18 June: Ukraine launched its largest ever drone attack on [Moscow](19004.md), targeting the [Moscow Refinery](77379611.md) and damaging several residential buildings.

  [^cite_note-426]

  [^cite_note-427]

  &#x20;Residents of Moscow reported oil [raining](19009110.md) down as massive plumes of black smoke were visible over the city.

  [^cite_note-428]

  &#x20;Following the attack, [Ukrainian president](20688479.md) [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) said that "If Ukraine burns, your Moscow will burn", warning against further Russian attacks on the country.

  [^cite_note-429]

- 24 June: Ukrainian forces launched long-range drone strikes in Russia, striking the [Orenburg Gas Processing Plant](42668669.md), which also housed the only [helium](13256.md) plant in Russia, and also striking two [Russian Satellite Communications Company](37243445.md) facilities, including one in [Moscow Oblast](372315.md) and another in [Vladimir Oblast](463919.md). Russia said [its forces](25709.md) shot down 323 Ukrainian drones overnight.

  [^cite_note-430]

- 26 June: Ukraine launched its largest drone attack on Russia since the start of the war, with over 660 drones launched at multiple [regions](701438.md), including Moscow Oblast, as [Ukrainian president](20688479.md) [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) said he had approved a "40-day campaign" aimed at "compelling Russia to end the war".

  [^cite_note-431]

- 28 June: Ukrainian forces launched [FP-5 Flamingo](80778495.md) [cruise missiles](6590.md) at strategic targets in Russia, striking the [Titan-Barrikady](54624831.md) complex in [Volgograd](32585.md), which produced launchers for Russia's [Yars](11487873.md) and [Iskander missile systems](5607520.md). Authorities said ten people had been wounded in the strikes. A [separate missile strike](71954377.md) destroyed a [Pantsir missile system](8645085.md) in [Crimea](163045.md).

  [^cite_note-432]

  [^cite_note-433]

- 30 June: Ukrainian forces launched a major drone attack on Moscow Oblast, striking the [Dubna Space Communications Center](37243445.md) to the north of Moscow. Russian authorities said a Ukrainian drone struck a residential building in [Yegoryevsk](2350904.md), killing an infant, while another drone strike reportedly killed a 61-year-old woman in [Tver Oblast](463952.md).

  [^cite_note-434]

### July 2026

- 19–23 July: Some 19 out of 71 petrol stations in [Belgorod](478861.md) have been damaged by Ukrainian drone strikes. Resulting in some 26.7% of the city's, and surrounding suburbs, fuel infrastructure being damaged.

  [^cite_note-435]

### August 2026

- 9 August: Ukrainian drones struck Belgorod, killing six people and damaging apartment buildings and other structures.

  [^cite_note-436]

- 10 August: Ukrainian drones struck the TANECO oil refinery in [Nizhnekamsk](2177679.md), [Tatarstan](67135.md), killing 13 people and damaging the refinery and nearby facilities.

  [^cite_note-437]

- 12 August: Ukrainian drones and missiles struck [Novorossiysk](1190336.md), a major Russian Black Sea port, damaging port infrastructure, air-defence systems and naval facilities; Russian authorities also reported civilian casualties and damage to a grain terminal.

  [^cite_note-438]

- 14–15 August: Ukrainian long-range strikes targeted Russian oil infrastructure and logistics facilities, including the [Ust-Luga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ust-Luga) oil terminal on the Baltic Sea.

  [^cite_note-439]

- 16 August: Ukrainian forces launched 822 drones at Russia, including more than 600 towards Moscow, killing at least seven people and injuring 39 others. A [Wildberries](58659019.md) warehouse was also destroyed.

  [^cite_note-440]

  &#x20;Strikes also caused casualties and fires in reported attacks on Russian military infrastructure in [Rostov](478744.md), [Samara](481962.md) and [Nizhny Novgorod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nizhny_Novgorod_Oblast).

  [^cite_note-441]

### September 2026

- 9 September: Ukrainian drones struck the [port of Makhachkala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Makhachkala) in Dagestan, the northernmost year-round ice-free port on the [Caspian Sea](19653787.md). Port infrastructure was damaged following multiple explosions and fires. There were no casualties according to Russian reports.

  [^cite_note-442]

  [^cite_note-443]

- 20 September: A major attack on Moscow with [FP-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fire_Point_FP-1) drones, [Flamingo cruise missiles](80778495.md) and first-use of a [Pelican ballistic missile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fire_Point_FP-7). Among other targets, the [Kapotnya Refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kapotnya_Refinery) was struck again, with multiple fires, and smoke rising that could be seen across the city. Moscow's mayor, Sergey Sobyanin, described the drone raid as the "largest ever", while stating that Russian air defenses intercepted over 1,600 drones in the previous day.

  [^cite_note-inkorr20260920-444]

  [^cite_note-abc20260920-445]

## Assassinations

- 20 August 2022, ultranationalist journalist, [political scientist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_scientist) and activist [Darya Dugina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darya_Dugina) was killed by a [car bombing](155282.md) in [Bolshiye Vyazyomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolshiye_Vyazyomy), [Odintsovsky District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odintsovsky_District), [Moscow Oblast](372315.md).

  [^cite_note-446]

- 2 April 2023, Russian pro-war military blogger '[Vladlen Tatarsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladlen_Tatarsky)' was [assassinated in a bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Vladlen_Tatarsky) in St Petersburg. The bomb, hidden inside a statuette and handed to him as a gift, exploded during an event he was hosting in a café. A Russian woman was later sentenced to 27 years in prison for the attack.

  [^cite_note-447]

- 6 May 2023, in the village of [Pionerskoye](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Pionerskoye,_Nyzhny_Novgorod\&action=edit\&redlink=1), [Bor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bor,_Nizhny_Novgorod_Oblast) District, [Nizhny Novgorod Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nizhny_Novgorod_Oblast), an anti-tank mine exploded under an [Audi Q7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Audi_Q7) car, in which the ultranationalist writer and politician [Zakhar Prilepin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zakhar_Prilepin) was driving. Prilepin received severe leg injuries, and his bodyguard died on the spot. Responsibility for the attack was claimed by [Atesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atesh_\(movement\)), a militant group of Ukrainians and [Crimean Tatars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crimean_Tatars).

  [^cite_note-448]

  [^cite_note-449]

- 11 July 2023, Navy Captain Stanislav Rzhitsky, deputy head of military mobilization efforts in [Krasnodar](474128.md), was shot and killed while jogging. As commander of the submarine *[Krasnodar](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Krasnodar_\(submarine\)\&action=edit\&redlink=1)* based in the [Black Sea](3386.md), he was accused of launching missiles that struck [Vinnytsia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vinnytsia) in [July 2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vinnytsia_missile_strikes) and killed 23 civilians, although his father claimed he had left active service prior to the invasion in 2021.

  [^cite_note-450]

- 16 November 2023, Oleksandr Slisarenko, the Russian-installed deputy head of the [Kharkov Military-Civilian Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_occupation_of_Kharkiv_Oblast), was killed in a car bombing reportedly carried out by the SBU in Belgorod.

  [^cite_note-451]

- 17 December 2024, Russian lieutenant general [Igor Kirillov](77324017.md) was assassinated in Moscow, the SBU claimed responsibility for the attack.

  [^cite_note-452]

- 3 February 2025, [Armen Sargsyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armen_Sargsyan), an Armenian-born paramilitary leader and founder of the Pro-Russian [Arbat Battalion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_irregular_units_in_Ukraine#Arbat_Battalion), was severely injured after a bomb detonated in the entrance hall of an apartment building in Moscow. He would later die due to his wounds in hospital.

  [^cite_note-453]

- 25 April 2025, Russian lieutenant general [Yaroslav Moskalik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yaroslav_Moskalik) was killed after a car bomb exploded in [Balashikha](1998682.md).

  [^cite_note-454]

- On 22 December 2025, General [Fanil Sarvarov](81906686.md) was killed with a bomb placed in a car.

  [^cite_note-455]

  &#x20;Two days later, a car exploded near the location of the previous assassination, killing two police officers and one suspect.

  [^cite_note-456]

## Ground incursions

### March 2023: Bryansk

This paragraph is an excerpt from [2023 Bryansk Oblast raid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Bryansk_Oblast_raid).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2023_Bryansk_Oblast_raid\&action=edit)]

On 2 March 2023, the [Russian](25391.md) authorities said that an armed [Ukrainian](31750.md) group crossed [the border](36430484.md) and attacked the villages of [Lyubechane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyubechane) and [Sushany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sushany) in [Bryansk Oblast](476473.md). Russia said the attackers fired on a car, killing two [civilians](145804.md), before the [Federal Security Service](385990.md) forced them back into Ukraine. The raid[^cite_note-2023_Bryansk_Oblast_raid_AP_2_March-457][^cite_note-458] was claimed by the [Russian Volunteer Corps](71652852.md); a [paramilitary group](146730.md) of Russian citizens, based in Ukraine, which opposes the [Russian regime of Vladimir Putin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russia_under_Vladimir_Putin).[^cite_note-indep-460] Russia called the incident a terrorist attack, and said its [9 March missile strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022%E2%80%932023_Russian_strikes_against_Ukrainian_infrastructure#March_2023) on Ukraine were retaliation. Ukraine's government denied involvement; it said the incident could have been a [false-flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False-flag) attack by Russia to justify its [ongoing war against Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine_\(2022%E2%80%93present\)), or else an attack by [anti-government partisans from within Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Belarusian_and_Russian_partisan_movement).&#x20;

### May–July 2023: Belgorod

Main article: [2023 Belgorod Oblast incursions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Belgorod_Oblast_incursions)



RVC members on 24 May 2023

On 22 May, another cross-border raid took place in Belgorod Oblast; in the [Kozinka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kozinka,_Belgorod_Oblast), [Gora-Podol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gora-Podol) and [Grayvoron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grayvoron) districts. The [Freedom of Russia Legion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_Russia_Legion) (FRL) and [Russian Volunteer Corps](71652852.md) (RVC) claimed responsibility for the attacks. A Ukrainian spokesperson, Andrii Yusov, made the same claim, stating that the attacks were to "liberate" the regions and to provide a buffer zone to protect Ukrainian civilians. Russian authorities attributed the attacks to "a Ukrainian sabotage-reconnaissance group".[^cite_note-461]&#x20;

On 1 June, the FRL and RVC launched another raid into Belgorod Oblast, this time near the small town of [Shebekino](4570394.md).[^cite_note-462]&#x20;

### March 2024: Belgorod and Kursk

Main article: [March 2024 western Russia incursion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_2024_western_Russia_incursion)

On 12 March 2024, the FRL and RVC launched raids into Kursk and Belgorod Oblasts, claiming control of [Tyotkino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyotkino) and [Lozovaya Rudka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lozovaya_Rudka).[^cite_note-463][^cite_note-464]&#x20;

### August 2024 – March 2025: Belgorod and Kursk

This paragraph is an excerpt from [Kursk campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kursk_campaign).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Kursk_campaign\&action=edit)]

On 6 August 2024, during the [Russo-Ukrainian war](70149799.md), the [Armed Forces of Ukraine](42799055.md) launched an incursion into [Russia](25391.md)'s [Kursk Oblast](480916.md) and clashed with the [Russian Armed Forces](25709.md) and [Russian border guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FSB_Border_Service_of_Russia).[^cite_note-Kursk_campaign_REU86-465][^cite_note-Kursk_campaign_dw\.com-466][^cite_note-Kursk_campaign_ft.com-467] A [state of emergency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_emergency_in_Russia) was declared in Kursk Oblast,[^cite_note-Kursk_campaign_BBC_8_Aug-468] and Russian reserves were rushed to the area.[^cite_note-Kursk_campaign_BBC_Cursino-469] By the end of the first week, the Ukrainian military said it had captured 1,000 km<sup>2</sup> (390 sq mi) of Russian territory, while Russian authorities acknowledged that Ukraine had captured 28 settlements.[^cite_note-470]&#x20;

&#x20;Starting on 18 March 2025, Ukrainian forces launched an incursion into Belgorod Oblast, near the towns of [Demidovka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demidovka,_Belgorod_Oblast), [Grafovka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grafovka) and [Prilesye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prilesye).[^cite_note-471][^cite_note-472]&#x20;

## Reactions

|  | This section needs to be **updated**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information. *(August 2024)* |
| --- | --- |

### Russia

In response to alleged shelling by Ukraine, Russia reportedly began reinforcing its border with Ukraine in November 2022, including building defense lines and fortifications along the border in Belgorod, Kursk and Bryansk regions.[^cite_note-473][^cite_note-474] A state of emergency was also declared in some villages and settlements along the border with Ukraine following repeated shelling attacks.[^cite_note-475]&#x20;

ISW reported in November 2022 that Russian forces could be preparing a large-scale false flag attack to gather popular support for the invasion, with Kremlin propagandists claiming that Ukraine is "preparing to invade" Belgorod Oblast.[^cite_note-english.nv.ua-476] A senior Ukrainian officer later said that Russia may attempt to escalate the situation on the border and claim "NATO and Ukrainian aggression". He cited fake news being spread in pro-Russian Telegram channels about "Ukrainian and NATO plans" to "attack peaceful Russian and Belarusian territory".[^cite_note-english.nv.ua-476]&#x20;

In May 2023, Russia's Security Council Secretary [Nikolai Patrushev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikolai_Patrushev) accused Ukraine's NATO allies of involvement in Ukrainian attacks in Russia, saying "the terrorist attacks committed in Russia are accompanied by an information campaign prepared in advance in Washington and London, designed to destabilise the socio-political situation, and to undermine the constitutional foundations and sovereignty of Russia."[^cite_note-477]&#x20;

After a Ukrainian [HIMARS](147011.md) strike destroyed a convoy of Russian troops in [Kursk Oblast](480916.md) in Russia on the night of 8–9 August 2024, Russian [milbloggers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milblog) responded with outrage. Many of them called for the commanders who authorized the movement of the column to be punished.[^cite_note-478] The Ukrainian attack on the convoy was described by Russian milbloggers as one of the bloodiest of the entire war.[^cite_note-479]&#x20;

Russian President [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) threatened retaliation for attacks on Russian territory.[^cite_note-480] He alleged that Western-supplied "long-range precision weapons cannot be used without space-based reconnaissance" and "highly skilled specialists" from NATO countries.[^cite_note-481] Experts said Putin's threats are aimed at dissuading the United States, the United Kingdom and France from allowing Ukraine to use Western-supplied long-range missiles such as the [ATACMS](147015.md) and [Storm Shadow](306908.md) in strikes against Russia.[^cite_note-482]&#x20;



Transportation of Russian citizens injured in the [crash of flight 8243](78712170.md) to Moscow

In December 2024, Vladimir Putin threatened to bring more "destruction" to Ukraine in retaliation for a Ukrainian drone attack on the central Russian city of [Kazan](202295.md) in the Republic of [Tatarstan](67135.md).[^cite_note-483]&#x20;

On 25 December 2024, the [Azerbaijan Airlines Flight 8243](78712170.md) crashed near [Aktau International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aktau_International_Airport), Kazakhstan, with 62 passengers and 5 crew on board. *Osprey Flight Solutions*, a United Kingdom-based aviation security firm which provides analysis for airlines still flying into Russia, warned its clients that the aircraft had likely been shot down by a Russian military air-defence system.[^cite_note-484] On the morning of 25 December, the head of the Security Council of the [Chechen Republic](6095.md), [Khamzat Kadyrov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khamzat_Kadyrov), confirmed that Grozny had been [attacked by drones](38212231.md).[^cite_note-Euronews_Exclusive-485] On 26 December, Azerbaijani government sources confirmed that a Russian surface-to-air missile, a [Pantsir-S1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pantsir-S1), had fired at the aircraft above [Grozny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grozny), detonating near the plane, injuring passengers and crew members. Despite the pilots' requests to perform an [emergency landing](1914622.md), they were denied to do so at any Russian airport, instead being ordered to fly towards Aktau.[^cite_note-Euronews_Exclusive-485]&#x20;

In response to the 18 June 2026 drone strikes on Moscow, the Russian government said that it will carry out a "massive retaliation" against Ukraine with [Russian foreign minister](1316703.md) [Sergey Lavrov](541346.md) saying the [Russian Armed Forces](25709.md) will from now on, carry out "massive group strikes" on a "regular basis" following the attack on Moscow.[^cite_note-486]&#x20;

### Ukraine

Ukrainian officials have rarely commented on the attacks. The Ukrainian military and intelligence services have acknowledged carrying out some of them, or have welcomed attacks without admitting involvement.&#x20;

In April 2022, [Mykhailo Podolyak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mykhailo_Podolyak), adviser to President [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md), referred to attacks in Russia as "karma" or payback for the Russian invasion. He said that Russian regions where fuel and weapons are stored were learning about "[demilitarization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/On_conducting_a_special_military_operation)", referring to one of Putin's stated goals of the invasion.[^cite_note-487]&#x20;

Ukraine's military denied taking part in the cross-border raids by the [Freedom of Russia Legion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_Russia_Legion) and [Russian Volunteer Corps](71652852.md). Andriy Yusov, a spokesperson for Ukraine's military intelligence, said that Russian citizens had launched the operations to liberate territories from the "Putin regime", to push Russian government forces away from the border and create a "security zone to protect the Ukrainian civilian population".[^cite_note-Guardian_23_May_23-488] Some Ukrainian commentators wryly compared them to Russia's past use of [proxy forces in Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_separatist_forces_in_Ukraine). Mykhailo Podolyak said "As you know, tanks are sold at any Russian military store", referencing Putin's claim that Russian troops and proxy forces [in 2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Donbas) were locals who bought their equipment at army surplus stores.[^cite_note-Guardian_23_May_23-488] Ukrainian social media accounts jokingly called the Belgorod region the "[Bilhorod People's Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bilhorod_People%27s_Republic)", in reference to the Russian-occupied [Donetsk](42425747.md) and [Luhansk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luhansk_People%27s_Republic) People's Republics. Another popular joke online referred to Putin announcing "another [Special Military Operation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Military_Operation) to defend Russian-speaking Russians from Russians invading Russia".[^cite_note-489]&#x20;

In response to Ukraine's [2024 cross-border offensive in Kursk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/August_2024_Kursk_Oblast_incursion), President Zelenskyy said that "Russia brought the war to our land and should feel what it has done".[^cite_note-490] He said: "Everything that inflicts losses on the Russian army, Russian state, their [military-industrial complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military-industrial_complex), and their economy helps prevent the war from expanding and brings us closer to a just end to this aggression".[^cite_note-491] Zelenskyy maintained that "Russia must be forced to make peace", adding that Ukrainian troops were helping to protect Ukraine by capturing areas from where Russia had launched numerous strikes.[^cite_note-492] Ukrainian foreign ministry spokesman Georgiy Tykhy said that "unlike Russia", Ukraine is not interested in taking the territory of its neighbor. Tykhy continued: "The sooner Russia agrees to restore a just peace ... the sooner the raids by the Ukrainian defense forces into Russia will stop".[^cite_note-493][^cite_note-494]&#x20;

### NATO

| \[icon] | This section **needs expansion** with: the UK's recent allowance of using Storm Shadow missiles to strike inside Russia. You can help by [adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Attacks_in_Russia_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war_\(2022%E2%80%93present\)\&action=edit\&section=)[adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Attacks_in_Russia_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war_\(2022%E2%80%93present\)\&action=edit\&section=) or [making an edit request](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Talk%3AAttacks_in_Russia_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war_%282022%E2%80%93present%29\&preload=Template%3ASubmit+an+edit+request%2Fpreload\&action=edit\&section=new\&editintro=Template%3AEdit+extended-protected%2Feditintro\&preloadtitle=Extended-confirmed-protected+edit+request+on+3+October+2026\&preloadparams%5B%5D=edit+extended-protected\&preloadparams%5B%5D=Attacks+in+Russia+during+the+Russo-Ukrainian+war+%282022%E2%80%93present%29). *(August 2024)* |
| --- | --- |

For more than two years, NATO states that [supplied Ukraine with weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_military_aid_to_Ukraine_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War), barred Ukraine from using those weapons to strike inside Russia.[^cite_note-495] US Secretary of State [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) said "We haven't encouraged or enabled strikes outside of Ukraine".[^cite_note-AP_29_May-496] However, in May 2024, NATO Secretary-General [Jens Stoltenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jens_Stoltenberg) and several European leaders said Ukraine should be allowed to use Western-supplied weapons to strike military targets inside Russia in self-defense. Stoltenberg said that "if \[Ukraine] cannot attack the Russian forces on the other side of the frontline because they are on the other side of the border, then of course you really reduce the ability of the Ukrainian forces to defend".[^cite_note-497] French President [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) said that Ukraine must be able to take out the bases across the border from where Russian missiles are fired, while German Chancellor [Olaf Scholz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olaf_Scholz) said Ukraine's defense "isn't limited to its territory".[^cite_note-AP_29_May-496] Italian Prime Minister [Giorgia Meloni](29228976.md) said she did not think it necessary to hit Russian military bases, and urged Western allies to supply Ukraine with more air defenses instead.[^cite_note-498] Italian Defense Minister [Guido Crosetto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guido_Crosetto) said it is "wrong to increase tension" in an already "dramatic" situation and underlined the need to leave open the possibility of "peace talks in the coming months".[^cite_note-499] Hungary's Foreign Minister [Peter Szijjarto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Szijjarto) said it was a "crazy idea because, as we've seen so far, the Russians will fire back".[^cite_note-500]&#x20;

On 30 May 2024, in response to Russia's [2024 Kharkiv offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Kharkiv_offensive), US President [Joe Biden](145422.md) gave Ukraine permission to strike the Russian military inside Russia with US-supplied weapons, but only to defend the [Kharkiv region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kharkiv_region) from "Russian forces hitting them or preparing to hit them".[^cite_note-501] The same permission was given to Ukraine by [Germany](11867.md),[^cite_note-502] [France](5843419.md) and the [United Kingdom](31717.md).[^cite_note-503] Dutch Foreign Minister [Hanke Bruins Slot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanke_Bruins_Slot) said that the [Netherlands](21148.md) would not object if Ukraine used Dutch-supplied [F-16 fighters](11642.md) to strike military targets inside Russia.[^cite_note-504]&#x20;

The US [Biden administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biden_administration) reportedly told Ukraine to stop attacking oil refineries in Russia, believing it would raise world energy prices and bring more aggressive Russian retaliation. Ukraine's government rejected the request.[^cite_note-505] The US also expressed concern at a Ukrainian drone strike against a Russian nuclear [early-warning radar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early-warning_radar), believing that it could dangerously unsettle the Russian government.[^cite_note-506]&#x20;

In July 2024, British Prime Minister [Keir Starmer](18681491.md) announced that the British government would authorize the use of [Storm Shadow](306908.md) long-range missiles against military targets in Russia.[^cite_note-507]&#x20;

On 17 November 2024, the Biden administration authorized the use of long-range [ATACMS](147015.md) missiles for military targets inside mainland Russia.[^cite_note-508][^cite_note-509]&#x20;
